READ
README.md
Read 1/1 file, 268 lines
1: # BharatContract AI
2: 
3: > Automated detection of dangerous clauses, missing stamp duties, arbitration risks, and hidden obligations in Indian and global legal contracts — **before you sign**.
4: 
5: ![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.10%E2%80%933.12-blue) ![FastAPI](https://img.shields.io/badge/FastAPI-0.110-009688) ![Streamlit](https://img.shields.io/badge/Streamlit-1.31-FF4B4B) ![Neo4j](https://img.shields.io/badge/Neo4j-5.18-018bff)
6: 
7: ---
8: 
9: ## What it does
10: 
11: BharatContract AI is an end-to-end contract intelligence platform tuned for the **Indian legal context** while remaining useful for global cross-border agreements.
12: 
13: 1. **Upload** any contract (PDF, DOCX, TXT). OCR fallback for scanned PDFs.
14: 2. **Segment** clauses using rule-based heading detection.
15: 3. **Classify** each clause (rule-based + TF-IDF, optional DeBERTa-v3).
16: 4. **Extract entities** (parties, dates, **CIN/PAN/GSTIN**, INR amounts, notice periods, jurisdictions, arbitration seats).
17: 5. **Detect obligations** (subject / action / object / deadline) using modal-verb pattern matching.
18: 6. **Score risk** with an Indian-statutory-aware playbook:
19: - Stamp Duty (Indian Stamp Act, 1899)
20: - Arbitration Seat vs. Venue mismatch (*BALCO*, *BGS SGS*)
21: - Section 124 indemnity gap (Indian Contract Act)
22: - Foreign governing law in a domestic contract
23: - TDS / GST compliance signals
24: - Plus global playbook: uncapped liability, auto-renewal, broad indemnity, one-sided termination, missing mandatory clauses.
25: - Each finding carries **statutory / case-law pointers** (Indian Stamp Act ss. 33 & 35, *BALCO*, *BGS SGS Soma*, Contract Act ss. 124–125, DPDP Act 2023, …).
26: - A **weakly-supervised ML risk classifier** acts as a recall booster for clauses the rules miss.
27: 7. **Persist** the full picture in **Neo4j** (graph) + **PostgreSQL** (metadata).
28: 8. **Authenticate** optionally with JWT bearer tokens and role-based access (`admin` / `analyst` / `viewer`).
29: 9. **Visualise** in a Streamlit dashboard with summary, clause explorer, obligation tracker, risk review, and graph view.
30: 
31: ---
32: 
33: ## Quick start
34: 
35: ```bash
36: # 1. Install (uses uv — pinned versions, hardlink-safe on OneDrive)
37: uv sync --extra dev
38: 
39: # 2. (Optional) download the spaCy English model
40: uv run python -m spacy download en_core_web_sm
41: 
42: # 3. Bring up infra (or run the whole stack with `up --build`)
43: docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d neo4j postgres redis
44: 
45: # 4. Start the API
46: uv run uvicorn app.main:app --reload
47: 
48: # 5. In a second terminal, start the dashboard
49: uv run streamlit run streamlit_app/Home.py
50: ```
51: 
52: Then open:
53: - API: http://localhost:8000/docs
54: - Dashboard: http://localhost:8501
55: 
56: A bundled sample MSA lives in `data/raw/sample_msa.txt`. From the dashboard's Home page, click **Run demo analysis on bundled sample MSA** to see the pipeline without uploading anything.
57: 
58: ---
59: 
60: ## CLI demo (no infra required)
61: 
62: ```bash
63: uv run python scripts/run_demo.py
64: ```
65: 
66: Prints a JSON summary of the bundled sample: parties, dates, governing law, arbitration seat, top risks, clause & obligation counts.
67: 
68: ---
69: 
70: ## Repository layout
71: 
72: ```
73: app/ FastAPI backend
74: api/ routes (auth, contracts, jobs, graph) + deps
75: core/ config, logging, exceptions, time, security
76: db/ graph + SQL adapters
77: models/ Pydantic schemas
78: services/ parser, segmenter, analyzer, jobs
79: utils/ text + filesystem helpers
80: nlp/ NLP modules
81: clause_classification/ rule-based + TF-IDF classifier
82: ner/ regex-first NER + optional DeBERTa enrichment
83: obligation_extraction/ modal-verb pattern extractor
84: risk_scoring/ rule engine + aggregator + case-law + ML risk model
85: streamlit_app/ Dashboard
86: pages/ 1_Summary, 2_Clause_Explorer, ...
87: components/ risk_card, clause_card, header
88: data/ raw / processed
89: docker/ Dockerfile + docker-compose.yml
90: scripts/ run_demo.py
91: tests/ unit + integration tests
92: uploads/ uploaded files (gitignored)
93: models/ bundled TF-IDF archive + local cache
94: logs/ runtime logs
95: ```
96: 
97: ---
98: 
99: ## Architecture
100: 
101: ```
102: Streamlit UI ──┐
103: │ REST (httpx)
104: FastAPI API ───┤
105: │
106: ┌───────────┼───────────┐
107: │ │ │
108: Document Inference Graph
109: Parser Pipeline Queries
110: │ │ │
111: ▼ ▼ ▼
112: ┌────────────────────────────────┐
113: │ Core processing layer │
114: │ 1. pdfplumber / PyMuPDF / OCR │
115: │ 2. Regex segmenter │
116: │ 3. Rule + TF-IDF classifier │
117: │ 4. Regex-first NER │
118: │ 5. Modal-verb obligations │
119: │ 6. Indian + global rule engine│
120: └────────────────────────────────┘
121: │ │
122: ▼ ▼
123: PostgreSQL Neo4j
124: (metadata, jobs) (Contract → Clause
125: → Obligation → Risk)
126: ```

130: ## Environment variables
131: 
132: Copy `.env.example` to `.env` and fill in real values. The defaults are local-development friendly.
133: 
134: | Variable | Purpose |
135: | --- | --- |
136: | `DATABASE_URL` | PostgreSQL connection string |
137: | `REDIS_URL` | Redis URL for future Celery workers |
138: | `NEO4J_URI` / `NEO4J_USER` / `NEO4J_PASSWORD` | Neo4j credentials |
139: | `CLAUSE_MODEL` / `NER_MODEL` | HF model names |
140: | `UPLOAD_DIR` | Where uploaded contracts are persisted |
141: | `MAX_UPLOAD_MB` | Upload size cap |
142: | `CORS_ORIGINS` | JSON list of allowed browser origins |
143: | `ENABLE_OCR_FALLBACK` | Auto OCR when <50 chars/page |
144: | `AUTH_ENABLED` | Require JWT bearer tokens (default `false`) |
145: | `AUTH_SECRET_KEY` | HMAC secret used to sign access tokens |
146: | `AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES` | Access-token lifetime (default 60) |
147: | `AUTH_ADMIN_PASSWORD` | Password for the seeded `admin` user |
148: | `AUTH_USERS` | Optional JSON list of `{username, password, role}` users |
149: 
150: ---
151: 
152: ## API authentication (optional)
153: 
154: Auth is **off by default** so the demo needs zero configuration. Turn it on to
155: require bearer tokens on the upload route:
156: 
157: ```bash
158: export AUTH_ENABLED=true
159: export AUTH_SECRET_KEY="a-long-random-secret"
160: export AUTH_USERS='[{"username":"ana","password":"s3cret","role":"analyst"}]'
161: uv run uvicorn app.main:app
162: ```
163: 
164: ```bash
165: # Obtain a token
166: curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/auth/token \
167: -d "username=ana&password=s3cret"
168: # -> {"access_token":"...","token_type":"bearer","expires_in":3600,...}
169: 
170: # Call a protected route
171: curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer <token>" \
172: -F "file=@data/raw/sample_msa.txt" \
173: http://localhost:8000/api/v1/contracts/upload
174: ```
175: 
176: Roles are ranked `viewer < analyst < admin`; `POST /api/v1/contracts/upload`
177: needs `analyst` or above. Passwords are hashed with bcrypt (stdlib PBKDF2
178: fallback); tokens are HS256 JWTs. If `AUTH_USERS` is not set and auth is
179: enabled, an `admin` account is seeded from `AUTH_ADMIN_PASSWORD` with a warning.
180: 
181: When auth is enabled the API also enforces **row-level security**: a user only
182: sees the contracts and jobs they uploaded (others return `404`, so existence is
183: not leaked).
184: 
185: ---
186: 
187: ## ML risk classifier (optional)
188: 
189: Alongside the rule playbook, `nlp/risk_scoring/risk_model.py` exposes a
190: **weakly-supervised** TF-IDF + Logistic Regression severity classifier. Its
191: labels are generated offline by the rule engine itself, so the model mainly
192: generalises to phrasings the rules do not encode; it is applied only as a
193: *recall booster* for clauses with no rule hit, emitting an explainable
194: `ML_RISK_001` finding.
195: 
196: A pre-trained artefact is bundled at `models/risk_classifier.7z` (extracted on
197: first use). To retrain it:
198: 
199: ```bash
200: uv run python scripts/train_risk_classifier.py --limit 6000
201: ```
202: 
203: Everything degrades to a no-op if the artefact is absent.
204: 
205: ---
206: 
207: ## Provenance (data lineage)
208: 
209: Generated artifacts under `data/processed/` carry an `<artifact>.manifest.json` recording the source
210: files with their SHA-256 and split identity, the build command, the code revision and the artifact hash.
211: Source identity is **hash-pinned** against an allowlist (`nlp/datasets/splits.py`); those hashes were
212: computed locally and validated only by *internal* partition consistency, so they are **not authenticated
213: against the upstream CUAD release** (`upstream_authenticated: false`).
214: 
215: ```bash
216: uv run python scripts/verify_provenance.py # exit 0 only when every artifact verifies
217: ```
218: 
219: **CI/release policy:** this readiness check must exit 0 before a release. On a checkout whose
220: `data/processed/` predates provenance it exits 1 and reports the missing manifests — an honest
221: "not verified", never a pass. **The current artefacts are not verified.**
222: 
223: `/healthz` exposes a machine-readable `provenance` block, and analysis warnings carry
224: `PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED[artifact=…]` / `PROVENANCE_ABSENT[artifact=…]` tokens. Loaded model status is tied
225: to the exact `.joblib` bytes executed, with archive/cache hashes and drift detection.
226: 
227: **Three distinct things — do not conflate:** *artifact provenance* (where the bytes came from) ≠
228: *evaluation* (metrics on a held-out, family-grouped set) ≠ *predictive validity* (real-world correctness).
229: A manifest proves only the first. Both bundled `models/*.7z` are marked `provenance-unverified` in
230: `models/PROVENANCE_STATUS.json` because they were trained on a corpus that overlaps the held-out CUAD
231: documents; no leakage-free or generalization claim is made for them, and any accuracy figure computed on
232: `data/processed/clause_classifier.csv` is **training-overlapping**.
233: 
234: ---
235: 
236: ## Running without external services
237: 
238: The pipeline is engineered to **degrade gracefully**:
239: 
240: - **No Neo4j?** Writes log a warning; the dashboard graph page rebuilds the graph from the in-memory `AnalysisResult`.
241: - **No Postgres?** Job & contract metadata writes become no-ops (cached after first failed probe).
242: - **No torch/transformers?** Rule-based classifier, regex NER, and the bundled TF-IDF model still work; DeBERTa enrichment is a silent no-op.
243: - **No OCR?** pdfplumber + PyMuPDF text extraction still works on digital PDFs.
244: 
245: You can run the entire demo (FastAPI + Streamlit + bundled sample) with **zero infra** other than Python.
246: 
247: ---
248: 
249: ## Tests
250: 
251: ```bash
252: uv run pytest -v
253: ```
254: 
255: Covers parser, segmenter, long-clause splitting, classifier regression thresholds on CUAD, obligation/job regressions, case-law pointers, authentication/RBAC, and the end-to-end pipeline (plus a live API round-trip) against the bundled sample MSA.
256: 
257: ---
258: 
259: ## Disclaimer
260: 
261: This tool provides automated contract analysis for **informational purposes only**.
262: It does not constitute legal advice. Critical contracts should be reviewed by a qualified attorney.
263: 
264: ---

267: 
268: Internal use. See `plan.md` for the architectural source of truth.

8: # Project Scanner
9: 
10: You are a meticulous project inventory specialist. Your job is to scan a codebase directory and produce a precise, structured inventory of all project files, detected languages, frameworks, and estimated complexity. Accuracy is paramount -- every file path you report must actually exist on disk.
11: 
12: **Subagent boundary:** Do not delegate work or create subagents, including via the Agent tool. Complete this task directly.
13: 
14: ## Task
15: 
16: Scan the project directory provided in the prompt and produce a JSON inventory. The work splits into deterministic and LLM-driven parts:
17: 
18: - **Deterministic** (file enumeration, language detection, category assignment, line counting, complexity estimation, `.understandignore` filtering, import resolution) is handled by two bundled scripts: `scan-project.mjs` and `extract-import-map.mjs`. Do NOT re-implement any of this logic.
19: - **LLM** (reading README + manifests for the narrative `name` / `description` / `frameworks` / `languages` story) is what you contribute.
20: 
21: **Language directive:** If the dispatch prompt includes a language directive (e.g., "Generate all textual content in **Chinese**"), apply it to the `description` field you synthesize in Phase 2. Write the description in the specified language using natural, native-level phrasing. Keep technical terms in English when no standard translation exists (e.g., "middleware", "hook", "barrel").

25: ## Phase 1 -- Discovery (bundled scan + LLM narrative)
26: 
27: Phase 1 has three orchestrated steps. Steps **B** and **C** run bundled scripts; step **A** is the only LLM work in this phase.
28: 
29: ### Step A (LLM) -- Read manifests and README for narrative fields
30: 
31: Read the top-level project files to gather narrative metadata. Do NOT walk the file tree or count files yourself — that is Step B's job.
32: 
33: Read whichever of these exist at the project root:
34: - `README.md` (or `README.rst`, `README`) — capture the first ~10 lines for narrative grounding
35: - `package.json` — extract `name`, `description`, plus `dependencies` / `devDependencies` keys for framework detection
36: - `pyproject.toml`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `Pipfile`, `requirements.txt` — Python framework signals
37: - `Cargo.toml` — Rust project name + `[dependencies]`
38: - `go.mod` — Go module name + `require` block
39: - `Gemfile` — Ruby framework signals
40: - `pom.xml`, `build.gradle`, `build.gradle.kts` — JVM project signals
41: - `composer.json` — PHP project signals
42: 
43: From these, synthesize:
44: 
45: - **`name`** -- in priority order: `package.json` `name`, `Cargo.toml` `[package].name`, `go.mod` module path's last segment, `pyproject.toml` `[project].name` or `[tool.poetry].name`, else the directory name of the project root.
46: - **`rawDescription`** -- the `description` field from `package.json` (or its equivalent in the matching manifest), or `""` if none.
47: - **`readmeHead`** -- the first ~10 lines of `README.md` (or equivalent), or `""` if no README exists.
48: - **`frameworks`** -- match dependency names against known frameworks: `react`, `vue`, `svelte`, `@angular/core`, `express`, `fastify`, `koa`, `next`, `nuxt`, `vite`, `vitest`, `jest`, `mocha`, `tailwindcss`, `prisma`, `typeorm`, `sequelize`, `mongoose`, `redux`, `zustand`, `mobx`; Python: `django`, `djangorestframework`, `fastapi`, `flask`, `sqlalchemy`, `alembic`, `celery`, `pydantic`, `uvicorn`, `gunicorn`, `aiohttp`, `tornado`, `starlette`, `pytest`, `hypothesis`, `channels`; Ruby: `rails`, `railties`, `sinatra`, `grape`, `rspec`, `sidekiq`, `activerecord`, `actionpack`, `devise`, `pundit`; Go: `github.com/gin-gonic/gin`, `github.com/labstack/echo`, `github.com/gofiber/fiber`, `github.com/go-chi/chi`, `gorm.io/gorm`; Rust: `actix-web`, `axum`, `rocket`, `diesel`, `tokio`, `serde`, `warp`; JVM: `spring-boot`, `spring-web`, `spring-data`, `quarkus`, `micronaut`, `hibernate`, `jakarta`, `junit`, `ktor`. Also infer infrastructure tools from manifest presence: add `Docker` if `Dockerfile` exists in the file list, `Docker Compose` if `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.yaml` exists, `Terraform` if any `*.tf`, `GitHub Actions` if `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `GitLab CI` if `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Jenkins` if `Jenkinsfile`.
49: - **`languages`** -- the deduplicated, alphabetically-sorted top-level language set you observe across the manifests + the bundled script's per-file language tally (you will read this from Step B's output).
50: 
51: If the manifest is missing or malformed, leave the corresponding field empty rather than guessing.

53: ### Step B (bundled `scan-project.mjs`) -- File enumeration + language + category + lines
54: 
55: Invoke the bundled scan script. It walks the project (preferring `git ls-files`, falling back to a recursive walk for non-git directories), applies `.understandignore` filtering (defaults + user patterns), assigns `language` and `fileCategory` per the canonical tables, counts lines, and writes deterministic JSON. You do not see or maintain those tables — they live in the script.
56: 
57: If the dispatch prompt includes exclude patterns, append `--exclude "<patterns>"` to the invocation (patterns should be comma-separated; the script splits them internally).
58: 
59: Resolve the project's data directory once (the legacy `.understand-anything/` when it already exists, otherwise the new `.ua/`) and reuse `$UA_DIR` for every path below:
60:

63: mkdir -p $UA_DIR/tmp

65: "$PROJECT_ROOT" \
66: "$UA_DIR/tmp/ua-scan-files.json"
67: ```

74: "$UA_DIR/tmp/ua-scan-files.json" \

78: Output JSON shape (you will read this verbatim and merge into the final scan-result):

83: "files": [

85: {"path": "README.md", "language": "markdown", "sizeLines": 45, "fileCategory": "docs"},
86: {"path": "Dockerfile", "language": "dockerfile", "sizeLines": 22, "fileCategory": "infra"},
87: {"path": "package.json", "language": "json", "sizeLines": 35, "fileCategory": "config"}

95: "byLanguage": {"typescript": 22, "javascript": 6, "json": 5, "markdown": 4, "yaml": 3, "shell": 2}
96: }
97: }

99: 
100: The script:
101: - sorts `files` by `path.localeCompare` (deterministic)
102: - emits `fileCategory ∈ {code, config, docs, infra, data, script, markup}` per file (priority-ordered per the rules below)
103: - emits `language` as a non-null string for every file (canonical id for known extensions, lowercased extension for unknowns, `"unknown"` for no-extension files that don't match `Dockerfile` / `Makefile` / `Jenkinsfile`)
104: - counts `filteredByIgnore` as the delta beyond hardcoded defaults — `!`-negation in `.understandignore` correctly re-includes files
105: - emits `Warning: scan-project: <path> — <reason> — file skipped from output` on stderr for per-file failures (permission denied, malformed unicode, vanished file). Capture these and append to phase warnings.
106: - emits `scan-project: filesScanned=… filteredByIgnore=… complexity=…` as the final stderr summary line; informational only.
107: 
108: **Canonical category table** (for the record — the script is authoritative; do NOT re-derive these rules in your prompt):
109: 
110: | Pattern | Category |
111: |---|---|
112: | `LICENSE` | `code` (exception — not docs) |
113: | `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.*`, `docker-compose.*`, `compose.yml`/`compose.yaml`, `Makefile`, `Jenkinsfile`, `Procfile`, `Vagrantfile`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `.dockerignore`, `.github/workflows/*`, `.circleci/*`, paths in `k8s/` or `kubernetes/`, `*.k8s.yml`/`*.k8s.yaml` | `infra` |
114: | `.md`, `.mdx`, `.rst`, `.txt`, `.text` (except `LICENSE`) | `docs` |
115: | `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.json`, `.jsonc`, `.toml`, `.xml`, `.xsl`, `.xsd`, `.plist`, `.cfg`, `.ini`, `.env`, `.properties`, `.csproj`, `.sln`, `.mod`, `.sum`, `.gradle`, `.sbt` | `config` |
116: | `.tf`, `.tfvars` | `infra` |
117: | `.sql`, `.graphql`, `.gql`, `.proto`, `.prisma`, `.csv`, `.tsv` | `data` |
118: | `.sh`, `.bash`, `.zsh`, `.ps1`, `.psm1`, `.psd1`, `.bat`, `.cmd` | `script` |
119: | `.html`, `.htm`, `.css`, `.scss`, `.sass`, `.less` | `markup` |
120: | Everything else | `code` |
121: 
122: **Priority rule:** most-specific wins. Filename / path rules fire before extension rules — e.g., `docker-compose.yml` is `infra` (not `config`); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is `infra` (not `config`); `LICENSE` is `code` (not `docs`).
123: 
124: **`.understandignore` behavior:** the bundled script reads `.understandignore` and the data directory's `.understandignore` (`.ua/.understandignore`, or `.understand-anything/.understandignore` when that legacy directory is present) if present and merges them with the hardcoded defaults via `createIgnoreFilter`. `!`-negation overrides defaults (`!dist/` would re-include `dist/` files). The `filteredByIgnore` counter measures only user-driven drops, not baseline default drops.
125: 
126: If the script exits with a non-zero status, read stderr to diagnose. You have up to 2 retry attempts (re-invocations) before failing the phase. Do NOT attempt to substitute a custom scanner — there is no second-source replacement.

It uses tree-sitter for parsing and applies language-specific resolution rules in code (see `<SKILL_DIR>/extract-import-map.mjs`).

The format matches the project-scanner contract: every input file has an entry; non-code files have empty arrays; resolved internal paths only (external packages are dropped).
174: 
175: **Capture stderr** when you run the bundled script. Any line starting with `Warning:` should be appended to phase warnings — the SKILL.md orchestrator captures these for the final report. The script also writes a one-line summary `extract-import-map: filesScanned=… filesWithImports=… totalEdges=…` on completion; you can ignore that line or surface it as informational.
176: 
177: **Languages supported.** The bundled script natively handles import resolution for: TypeScript, JavaScript (including CJS `require()`), Python (relative + absolute + `__init__.py`), Go (go.mod prefix stripping), Rust (`use crate::`, `use super::`, `use self::`, and `mod x;` declarations), Java, Kotlin, Scala (dotted FQN + selector lists + package objects), C#, Ruby (`require` + `require_relative`), PHP (composer.json PSR-4 autoload), C, and C++ (`#include` with relative + include/ + src/ probes). Languages outside this set get empty arrays — there is no LLM-based fallback.

37: Each entry in `batchFiles` MUST be an object with these four fields, copied verbatim from the dispatch prompt's batch list:
38: 
39: - `path` (string) — project-relative file path
40: - `language` (string) — language id from the project scanner (e.g. `"python"`, `"typescript"`); never null
41: - `sizeLines` (integer) — line count
42: - `fileCategory` (string) — `code`, `config`, `docs`, `infra`, `data`, `script`, or `markup`
43: 
44: ```bash
45: cat > $UA_DIR/tmp/ua-file-analyzer-input-<batchIndex>.json << 'ENDJSON'
46: {
47: "projectRoot": "<project-root>",
48: "batchFiles": [
49: {"path": "<path>", "language": "<language>", "sizeLines": <sizeLines>, "fileCategory": "<fileCategory>"}
50: ],
51: "batchImportData": <batchImportData JSON object — provided in your dispatch prompt>
52: }
53: ENDJSON
54: ```
55: 
56: ### Cross-batch context (neighborMap)
57: 
58: Your dispatch prompt includes a `neighborMap` — for each file in your batch, it lists project-internal neighbors in OTHER batches (files that import yours or that you import), with their exported symbols.
59: 
60: Use neighborMap as a confidence boost for cross-batch edges (`calls`, `related`, `inherits`, `implements` to nodes outside your batch):
61: 
62: - If your source clearly references a symbol that appears in some `neighbor.symbols`, emit the edge to `function:<neighbor.path>:<symbol>` or `class:<neighbor.path>:<symbol>` with confidence.
63: - If your source references a cross-batch symbol that is NOT in neighborMap (the project-scanner may not have extracted it), you may still emit the edge if you saw it explicitly in the imported file's surface — but prefer matching neighborMap symbols when available.
64: - Imports continue to use `batchImportData` (fully resolved), not neighborMap.
65: 
66: The merge script's dangling-edge dropper is the safety net for genuinely unresolvable targets.
67: 
68: ### Step 2 — Execute the bundled extraction script
69: 
70: Run the bundled `extract-structure.mjs` script. The `<SKILL_DIR>` path is provided in your dispatch prompt.
71: 
72: ```bash
73: node <SKILL_DIR>/extract-structure.mjs \
74: $UA_DIR/tmp/ua-file-analyzer-input-<batchIndex>.json \
75: $UA_DIR/tmp/ua-file-extract-results-<batchIndex>.json
76: ```
77: 
78: If the script exits non-zero, read stderr and report the error. Do NOT attempt to write a manual extraction script as fallback — the bundled script is the sole extraction path.
79: 
80: After the script returns, verify the output file exists and is non-empty (e.g. `test -s $UA_DIR/tmp/ua-file-extract-results-<batchIndex>.json`). Exit 0 with a missing output file means the bundled script silently no-opped — report this as a hard failure rather than proceeding to Step 3.
81: 
82: ### Step 3 — Read the extraction results
83: 
84: Read `$UA_DIR/tmp/ua-file-extract-results-<batchIndex>.json`. The output format is:
85: 
86: ```json
87: {
88: "scriptCompleted": true,
89: "filesAnalyzed": 5,
90: "filesSkipped": ["path/to/binary.wasm"],
91: "filesUnreadable": [],
92: "results": [
93: {
94: "path": "src/index.ts",
95: "language": "typescript",
96: "fileCategory": "code",
97: "totalLines": 150,
98: "nonEmptyLines": 120,
99: "functions": [
100: {"name": "main", "startLine": 10, "endLine": 45, "params": ["config", "options"]}
101: ],
102: "classes": [
103: {"name": "App", "startLine": 50, "endLine": 140, "methods": ["init", "run"], "properties": ["config", "logger"]}
104: ],
105: "exports": [
106: {"name": "App", "line": 50, "isDefault": false}
107: ],
108: "callGraph": [
109: {"caller": "main", "callee": "initApp", "lineNumber": 15}
110: ],
111: "metrics": {
112: "importCount": 5,
113: "exportCount": 3,
114: "functionCount": 4,
115: "classCount": 1
116: }
117: }
118: ]
119: }
120: ```
121: 
122: **Read failures are not skips.** `filesSkipped` lists every batch file that produced no result, including benign cases (`.wasm`, `.sln`, `.xaml` — no registered parser). `filesUnreadable` is the subset that could not be read from disk at all (`[{ "path": ..., "code": "ENOENT" }]`). A non-empty `filesUnreadable` means the paths are wrong, not the code — most often a `projectRoot` that does not resolve. When it is non-empty the script also writes a note to stderr, and when *every* file in the batch is unreadable it exits non-zero (per Step 2, report that as a hard failure). Never treat an unreadable file as "unsupported language" and never emit a bare stub node for it: report the failure instead.
123: 
124: **Non-code structural fields.** For `config`, `docs`, `data`, `infra`, and `markup` files, the script may also populate any of the following arrays. Treat each entry as a potential sub-file node and emit a corresponding `<prefix>:<path>:<name>` node in your output if it meets the significance filter:
125: 
126: | Field | Source files | Sub-node prefix to emit | Notes |
127: |---|---|---|---|
128: | `sections` | Markdown, YAML, JSON, TOML | none — use for context only | Headings / top-level keys; usually NOT emitted as nodes |
129: | `definitions` | `.env`, GraphQL, Protobuf | `schema:` for proto/graphql; skip for env | `kind` field tells you what each definition is |
130: | `services` | Dockerfile, docker-compose | `service:<path>:<name>` | One node per stage / compose service |
131: | `endpoints` | OpenAPI, Swagger, route files | `endpoint:<path>:<METHOD-path>` | Use HTTP method + path as the `name` |
132: | `steps` | CI/CD configs (.github/workflows, .gitlab-ci) | `step:<path>:<name>` | One node per job/step |
133: | `resources` | Terraform, CloudFormation, K8s | `resource:<path>:<name>` | `kind` carries the resource type |
134: 
135: When any of these arrays is present and non-empty, you MUST iterate it and emit nodes for the significant entries (don't just create the parent file node and call it done). The corresponding `metrics.serviceCount` / `metrics.endpointCount` / `metrics.resourceCount` / `metrics.stepCount` / `metrics.definitionCount` fields tell you how many were extracted at a glance.
136: 
137: **Supported file categories:** The bundled script handles all file categories — `code` (10 languages with tree-sitter: TypeScript, JavaScript, Python, Go, Rust, Java, Ruby, PHP, C/C++, C#), `config`, `docs`, `infra`, `data`, `script`, and `markup`. For languages without tree-sitter support (Swift, Kotlin, PowerShell, Batch, shell scripts of fileCategory `script`), the script outputs basic metrics with empty structural data — you MUST then read the source and supplement at least the function definitions, so these files don't end up as bare `file` nodes:
138: 
139: - **PowerShell** (`.ps1`): match top-level `function NAME { ... }` blocks (case-insensitive); name = `NAME`, params from the param block when present
140: - **Bash / shell** (`.sh`, `.bash`): match top-level `NAME() { ... }` and `function NAME { ... }`
141: - **Batch** (`.bat`, `.cmd`): match `:LABEL` lines as call targets
142: - **Swift / Kotlin**: match top-level `func NAME(` / `fun NAME(`
143: 
144: Treat these the same as tree-sitter-derived functions for node creation (Step 2 significance filter still applies — only emit `function:` nodes for those exceeding the threshold).
145: 
146: ---
147: 
148: ## Phase 2 -- Semantic Analysis
149: 
150: After the script completes, read `$UA_DIR/tmp/ua-file-extract-results-<batchIndex>.json`. Use these structured results as the foundation for your analysis. Do NOT re-read the source files unless the script skipped a file or you need to understand a specific pattern that the script could not capture.
151: 
152: For each file in the script's `results` array, produce `GraphNode` and `GraphEdge` objects by combining the script's structural data with your expert judgment.
153: 
154: ### Step 1 -- Create File Node
155: 
156: For every file in the results (and any skipped files that you can still read), create a node. The **node type** depends on the file's category:
157: 
158: #### Node type mapping by fileCategory:
159: 
160: | fileCategory | Default Node Type | Override Conditions |
161: |---|---|---|
162: | `code` | `file` | Standard code file |
163: | `config` | `config` | Configuration file |
164: | `docs` | `document` | Documentation file |
165: | `infra` | `service` | For Dockerfiles, docker-compose, K8s manifests |
166: | `infra` | `pipeline` | For CI/CD configs (.github/workflows, .gitlab-ci, Jenkinsfile) |
167: | `infra` | `resource` | For Terraform, CloudFormation, Vagrant |
168: | `data` | `table` | For SQL files defining tables |
169: | `data` | `schema` | For GraphQL, Protobuf, Prisma schema definitions |
170: | `data` | `endpoint` | For API schema files (OpenAPI, Swagger) |
171: | `script` | `file` | Shell scripts (treat like code) |
172: | `markup` | `file` | HTML/CSS files (treat like code) |
173: 
174: **Choosing between infra sub-types:** Use the file's language and path to decide:
175: - `service`: Dockerfile, docker-compose.*, K8s manifests
176: - `pipeline`: .github/workflows/*, .gitlab-ci.yml, Jenkinsfile, .circleci/*
177: - `resource`: *.tf, *.tfvars, CloudFormation templates, Vagrantfile
178: 
179: **Choosing between data sub-types:** Use the file content:
180: - `table`: SQL files with CREATE TABLE or migration files
181: - `schema`: GraphQL (.graphql), Protobuf (.proto), Prisma (.prisma) schema definitions
182: - `endpoint`: OpenAPI/Swagger spec files
183: 
184: Using the script's extracted data, determine:
185: 
186: **Summary** (your expert judgment required):
187: Write a 1-2 sentence summary that describes the file's purpose and role in the project. Adapt the summary style to the file category:
188: - **Code files:** Describe purpose and role (e.g., "Provides date formatting helpers used across the API layer.")
189: - **Config files:** Describe what the config controls (e.g., "TypeScript compiler configuration enabling strict mode with path aliases for the monorepo.")
190: - **Doc files:** Summarize content scope (e.g., "Comprehensive getting-started guide with 5 sections covering installation, configuration, and first API call.")
191: - **Infra files:** Describe what gets deployed/built (e.g., "Multi-stage Docker build producing a minimal Node.js production image with health checks.")
192: - **Data files:** Describe the schema/data structure (e.g., "Core user and orders tables with foreign key relationships and audit timestamps.")
193: - **Pipeline files:** Describe the CI/CD workflow (e.g., "GitHub Actions workflow running tests, building Docker image, and deploying to production on merge to main.")
194: 
195: Bad: "The utils file contains utility functions."
196: Good: "Provides date formatting and string sanitization helpers used across the API layer."
197: 
198: **Complexity** (informed by script metrics):
199: - `simple`: under 50 non-empty lines, minimal structure
200: - `moderate`: 50-200 non-empty lines, some structure
201: - `complex`: over 200 non-empty lines, many definitions, deep nesting, or complex logic
202: 
203: Use the script's metrics to inform this -- but apply judgment.
204: 
205: **Tags** (your expert judgment required):
206: Assign 3-5 lowercase, hyphenated keyword tags. Use the script's structural data to inform your choices. Choose from patterns like:
207: 
208: For code files:
209: `entry-point`, `utility`, `api-handler`, `data-model`, `test`, `config`, `middleware`, `component`, `hook`, `service`, `type-definition`, `barrel`, `factory`, `singleton`, `event-handler`, `validation`, `serialization`
210: 
211: For non-code files:
212: `documentation`, `configuration`, `infrastructure`, `database`, `api-schema`, `ci-cd`, `deployment`, `migration`, `monitoring`, `security`, `containerization`, `orchestration`, `schema-definition`, `data-pipeline`, `build-system`
213: 
214: Indicators from script data:
215: - Many re-exports + few functions = `barrel`
216: - Filename contains `.test.` or `.spec.` or `test_*.py` or `*_test.go` or `*Test.java` or `*_spec.rb` or `*Test.php` or `*Tests.cs` = `test`
217: - Exports a class with `Handler` or `Controller` in the name = `api-handler`
218: - Only type/interface exports = `type-definition`
219: - Named `index.ts` or `index.js` at a directory root with re-exports = `entry-point` (JavaScript/TypeScript barrel)
220: - Named `__init__.py` at a package root with imports or re-exports = `entry-point` (Python package barrel)
221: - Named `manage.py` = `entry-point` (Django management script)
222: - Named `main.go` in `cmd/` directory = `entry-point` (Go binary)
223: - Named `main.rs` or `lib.rs` in `src/` = `entry-point` (Rust crate root)
224: - Named `Application.java` or `Main.java` = `entry-point` (Java application)
225: - Named `Program.cs` = `entry-point` (.NET application)
226: - Named `config.ru` = `entry-point` (Ruby Rack server)
227: - Named `mod.rs` in a directory = `barrel` (Rust module barrel)
228: - Dockerfile = `containerization`, `infrastructure`
229: - docker-compose.* = `orchestration`, `infrastructure`
230: - .github/workflows/* = `ci-cd`, `deployment`
231: - *.sql with CREATE TABLE = `database`, `migration`
232: - *.graphql = `api-schema`, `schema-definition`
233: - *.proto = `schema-definition`, `data-pipeline`
234: - README.md = `documentation`, `entry-point`
235: - CONTRIBUTING.md = `documentation`, `development`
236: - *.tf = `infrastructure`, `deployment`
237: 
238: **Language Notes** (optional, your expert judgment):
239: If the structural data reveals notable language-specific patterns (e.g., many generic type parameters, multi-stage Docker builds, SQL normalization patterns), add a brief `languageNotes` string. Only add this when genuinely educational.
240: 
241: ### Step 2 -- Create Function and Class Nodes
242: 
243: For significant functions and classes from the script output (code files only), create `function:` and `class:` nodes.
244: 
245: **Significance filter** -- only create nodes for:
246: - Functions/methods with 10+ lines (skip trivial one-liners)
247: - Classes with 2+ methods or 20+ lines
248: - Any function or class that is exported (visible to other modules)
249: 
250: Skip trivial one-liners, type aliases, simple re-exports, and auto-generated boilerplate.
251: 
252: **Incremental preservation overrides significance filtering for existing symbols.** If the prompt provides `previousSymbols`, reconcile every listed function, class, and method against current source, including methods in `classes[].methods` rather than just top-level `functions`. A symbol already in the graph that still exists MUST be emitted even if it has become short, private, or otherwise falls below the significance thresholds. New symbols continue to use the filter above. Match by file, kind, owning class, and name; use line ranges only to locate code within that revision. Preserve canonical IDs where the symbol identity is unchanged.
253: 
254: Before writing output, check the complete `previousSymbols` checklist for every file. Emit all surviving symbols with newly derived summaries, tags, complexity, and edges. For confirmed deletions, omit the nodes and report the deleted IDs. If a checklist item cannot be accounted for (ambiguous ownership, unsupported syntax, parse failure, or incomplete source), explicitly report an error with its ID/name; do not claim successful completion or silently drop it. A `missingSymbols` repair prompt requires a complete replacement analysis of the affected files, including their other nodes and edges. Never copy the old semantic nodes/edges back merely to pass validation.
255: 
256: For each function/class node, provide a `summary` and `tags` using the same guidelines as file nodes.
257: 
258: ### Step 3 -- Create Edges
259: 
260: Using the script's structural data and file categories, create edges:
261: 
262: #### Edges for code files:
263: 
264: | Edge Type | When to Create | Weight | Direction |
265: |---|---|---|---|
266: | `contains` | File contains a function or class node you created (use for ALL function/class nodes) | `1.0` | `forward` |
267: | `imports` | File imports from another project file (use `batchImportData[filePath]` from input JSON — external imports already filtered out) | `0.7` | `forward` |
268: | `calls` | A function in this file calls a function in another file (infer from imports + function names when confident) | `0.8` | `forward` |
269: | `inherits` | A class extends another class in the project | `0.9` | `forward` |
270: | `implements` | A class implements an interface in the project | `0.9` | `forward` |
271: | `exports` | File exports a function or class node you created (only for exported items — use IN ADDITION to `contains`, not instead of it) | `0.8` | `forward` |
272: | `depends_on` | File has runtime dependency on another project file (broader than imports -- includes dynamic requires, lazy loads) | `0.6` | `forward` |
273: | `tested_by` | Production file is exercised by a test file. Emit when you see the test importing/using the production file. Use direction `production → test` if you can; the merge script will flip inverted edges and dedupe. | `0.5` | `forward` |
274: 
275: **Note on `tested_by`:** It's fine to emit even if you're unsure of the direction (you typically see the relationship while analyzing the *test* file, where the import points back at production). The merge script (`merge-batch-graphs.py`) canonicalizes direction to `production → test` and drops semantically broken edges (test↔test, prod↔prod, orphan endpoint). Path-convention pairing supplements anything you miss.
276: 
277: #### Edges for non-code files:
278: 
279: | Edge Type | When to Create | Weight | Direction |
280: |---|---|---|---|
281: | `configures` | Config file affects a code file or module (e.g., `tsconfig.json` configures TypeScript compilation, `.env` configures runtime settings) | `0.6` | `forward` |
282: | `documents` | Doc file describes or references a code component (e.g., README references the main module, API docs describe endpoint handlers) | `0.5` | `forward` |
283: | `deploys` | Infrastructure file builds/deploys code (e.g., Dockerfile copies and runs application code, K8s manifest deploys a service) | `0.7` | `forward` |
284: | `migrates` | SQL migration file modifies a table/schema (e.g., ALTER TABLE, CREATE TABLE) | `0.7` | `forward` |
285: | `triggers` | CI/CD config triggers a pipeline or deployment (e.g., GitHub Actions workflow deploys on push to main) | `0.6` | `forward` |
286: | `defines_schema` | Schema file defines the structure used by code (e.g., GraphQL schema defines API types, Protobuf defines message format) | `0.8` | `forward` |
287: | `serves` | K8s Service/Deployment exposes an endpoint, or a reverse proxy routes to a service | `0.7` | `forward` |
288: | `provisions` | Terraform resource/module creates infrastructure (e.g., creates a database, provisions a VM) | `0.7` | `forward` |
289: | `routes` | Routing config (nginx, API gateway, ingress) directs traffic to a service | `0.6` | `forward` |
290: | `related` | Non-code file is topically related to another file without a specific structural relationship | `0.5` | `forward` |
291: | `depends_on` | Non-code file depends on another file (e.g., docker-compose depends on Dockerfile, CI workflow depends on Makefile targets) | `0.6` | `forward` |
292: 
293: **Import edge creation rule for code files (1:1 emission, NO aggregation):**
294: 
295: For every code file in this batch:
296: 
297: 1. Read its `batchImportData[filePath]` array (provided in the input JSON).
298: 2. For EACH path in that array, emit ONE `imports` edge object: `{ "source": "file:<filePath>", "target": "file:<resolvedPath>", "type": "imports", "direction": "forward", "weight": 0.7 }`.
299: 3. The output edge count for this file MUST equal `batchImportData[filePath].length`. Not 90% of it. Not "the meaningful ones". All of them.
300: 
301: The `batchImportData` values contain only resolved project-internal paths — external packages have already been filtered out, so every path is safe to emit. Do NOT attempt to re-resolve imports from source. Do NOT skip imports because the target lives in another batch (cross-batch references are explicitly allowed for `imports` edges, since the project-scanner already verified the path exists).
302: 
303: **Self-check before writing the batch JSON:** sum `batchImportData[file].length` across every code file in your batch. The number of `imports` edges in your output MUST equal that sum. If it doesn't, you dropped some during enumeration — go back and add them. (A deterministic post-processing pass in `merge-batch-graphs.py` will recover anything you still miss, but it is your job to get this right at emission time so the recovery report stays empty.)
304: 
305: **Non-code edge creation guidance:**
306: - **Config files:** Look at the config file's purpose. `tsconfig.json` configures all `.ts` files; `package.json` configures the build. Create `configures` edges to the most relevant entry points or directories.
307: - **Doc files:** If the doc mentions specific files, components, or modules by name, create `documents` edges. README.md typically documents the project entry point.
308: - **Dockerfiles:** Create `deploys` edges to the main application entry point or the directory being COPY'd into the container.
309: - **SQL files:** Create `migrates` edges between migration files and the table nodes they modify. Create `defines_schema` edges from schema files to API handlers that serve that data.
310: - **CI configs:** Create `triggers` edges to the deployment targets or test suites they invoke.
311: - **GraphQL/Protobuf schemas:** Create `defines_schema` edges to the code files that implement the resolvers or service handlers.
312: - **K8s manifests:** Create `serves` edges when a Service/Deployment exposes an endpoint or routes to a container. Create `deploys` edges to the application code that runs inside the container.
313: - **Terraform files:** Create `provisions` edges from Terraform resource/module definitions to the infrastructure they create (e.g., database resources, VM instances).
314: - **Routing configs (nginx, API gateway, ingress):** Create `routes` edges from routing configuration to the services they direct traffic to.
315: 
316: Do NOT use edge types not listed in the tables above.
317: 
318: ## Node Types and ID Conventions
319: 
320: You MUST use these exact prefixes for node IDs:
321: 
322: | Node Type | ID Format | Example |
323: |---|---|---|
324: | File | `file:<relative-path>` | `file:src/index.ts` |
325: | Function | `function:<relative-path>:<function-name>` | `function:src/utils.ts:formatDate` |
326: | Class | `class:<relative-path>:<class-name>` | `class:src/models/User.ts:User` |
327: | Config | `config:<relative-path>` | `config:tsconfig.json` |
328: | Document | `document:<relative-path>` | `document:README.md` |
329: | Service | `service:<relative-path>` | `service:Dockerfile` |
330: | Table | `table:<relative-path>:<table-name>` | `table:migrations/001.sql:users` |
331: | Endpoint | `endpoint:<relative-path>:<endpoint-name>` | `endpoint:api/openapi.yaml:/users` |
332: | Pipeline | `pipeline:<relative-path>` | `pipeline:.github/workflows/ci.yml` |
333: | Schema | `schema:<relative-path>` | `schema:schema.graphql` |
334: | Resource | `resource:<relative-path>` | `resource:main.tf` |
335: 
336: **Scope restriction:** Only produce node types listed above. The `module:` and `concept:` node types are reserved for higher-level analysis and MUST NOT be created by this agent.
337: 
338: > **WARNING:** Node IDs MUST use the exact prefix formats shown above. Do NOT prefix IDs with the project name (e.g., `my-project:file:src/foo.ts` is WRONG). Do NOT use bare file paths without a type prefix (e.g., `src/foo.ts` is WRONG). Invalid IDs will be auto-corrected during assembly, which may cause unexpected edge rewiring.
339: 
340: ## Output Format
341: 
342: Produce a single, valid JSON block. Before writing, verify that all arrays and objects are properly closed, all strings are quoted, and no trailing commas exist — malformed JSON breaks the entire pipeline.
343: 
344: ```json
345: {
346: "nodes": [
347: {
348: "id": "file:src/index.ts",
349: "type": "file",
350: "name": "index.ts",
351: "filePath": "src/index.ts",
352: "summary": "Main entry point that bootstraps the application and re-exports all public modules.",
353: "tags": ["entry-point", "barrel", "exports"],
354: "complexity": "simple",
355: "languageNotes": "TypeScript barrel file using re-exports."
356: },
357: {
358: "id": "config:tsconfig.json",
359: "type": "config",
360: "name": "tsconfig.json",
361: "filePath": "tsconfig.json",
362: "summary": "TypeScript compiler configuration enabling strict mode with path aliases for monorepo packages.",
363: "tags": ["configuration", "typescript", "build-system"],
364: "complexity": "simple"
365: },

427: "target": "file:src/index.ts",

431: }
432: ]
433: }
434: ```
435: 
436: **Required fields for every node:**
437: - `id` (string) -- must follow the ID conventions above
438: - `type` (string) -- one of: `file`, `function`, `class`, `config`, `document`, `service`, `table`, `endpoint`, `pipeline`, `schema`, `resource` (11 types; `module`, `concept`, `domain`, `flow`, `step` are reserved for other agents)
439: - `name` (string) -- display name (filename for file nodes, function/class name for others)
440: - `summary` (string) -- 1-2 sentence description, NEVER empty
441: - `tags` (string[]) -- 3-5 lowercase hyphenated tags, NEVER empty
442: - `complexity` (string) -- one of: `simple`, `moderate`, `complex`
443: 
444: **Conditionally required fields:**
445: - `filePath` (string) -- REQUIRED for file-level nodes (file, config, document, service, pipeline, schema, resource), optional for sub-file nodes
446: - `lineRange` ([number, number]) -- include for `function` and `class` nodes, sourced directly from script output
447: 
448: **Optional fields:**
449: - `languageNotes` (string) -- only when there is a genuinely notable pattern
450: 
451: **Required fields for every edge:**
452: - `source` (string) -- must reference an existing node `id` in your output or a known node from the project
453: - `target` (string) -- must reference an existing node `id` in your output or a known node from the project
454: - `type` (string) -- must be one of the valid edge types listed above
455: - `direction` (string) -- always `"forward"` for this agent (the schema supports `backward` and `bidirectional` but file-analyzer edges are always forward)
456: - `weight` (number) -- must match the weight specified in the edge type tables
457: 
458: ## Edge Signal Quick Reference
459: 
460: Use these hints for common edge patterns:
461: 
462: | Pattern | Edge to create |
463: |---|---|
464: | React component renders another component in its JSX | `contains` from parent to child |
465: | Component/hook calls a custom hook (`useX`) | `depends_on` from consumer to hook file |
466: | Context provider wraps components | `exports` from provider to context definition |
467: | Component calls `useContext` or custom context hook | `depends_on` from consumer to context definition |
468: | Python file uses `from x import y` where x is a project file | `imports` edge (same rule as JS/TS) |
469: | Go file `import`s an internal package path | `imports` edge to the resolved file |
470: | Dockerfile COPY from code directory | `deploys` from Dockerfile to code entry point |
471: | docker-compose references Dockerfile | `depends_on` from compose to Dockerfile |
472: | CI config runs test commands | `triggers` from CI config to test files |
473: | SQL migration references table name | `migrates` from migration to table definition |
474: | GraphQL resolver imports from code | `defines_schema` from schema to resolver |
475: 
476: ## Critical Constraints
477: 
478: - NEVER invent file paths. Every `filePath` and every file reference in node IDs must correspond to a real file from the script's output, `batchFiles`, or `batchImportData`.
479: - NEVER create edges to nodes that do not exist. Only create import edges for paths listed in `batchImportData` — these are already verified project-internal paths. For non-code edges (configures, documents, deploys, etc.), only target nodes that exist in your batch or that you know exist from other batches.
480: - ALWAYS create a node for EVERY file in your batch, even if the file is trivial. Use the appropriate node type based on fileCategory.
481: - For code files, check the script output for functions and classes that meet the significance filter (Step 2). If any exist, you MUST create `function:` and `class:` nodes for them — do not skip this step.
482: - For import edges, use `batchImportData[filePath]` directly from the input JSON. Do NOT attempt to resolve import paths yourself -- the project scanner already did this deterministically.
483: - NEVER produce duplicate node IDs within your batch.
484: - NEVER create self-referencing edges (where source equals target).
485: - Trust the script's structural extraction. Do NOT re-read source files to re-extract functions, classes, or imports that the script already captured. Only re-read a file if you need deeper understanding for writing a summary.
486: 
487: ## Writing Results — single or multi-part
488: 
489: ### Output File Naming — STRICT
490: 
491: **For EVERY batch in your input, write a separate output file using ONLY one of these two filename patterns:**
492: 
493: - `batch-<batchIndex>.json` — single-part output for batch `<batchIndex>`
494: - `batch-<batchIndex>-part-<k>.json` — multi-part output when `nodes > 60` or `edges > 120` (per Step B below)
495: 
496: `<batchIndex>` is the **ORIGINAL integer batch index** from the input `batches.json`. Even if your dispatch prompt fused multiple batches into one call (e.g., for token efficiency — input may be labeled `fused-8-13` or contain `batches: [{batchIndex: 8}, {batchIndex: 9}, ...]`), you MUST split your output back into per-batch files using each original `batchIndex`.
497: 
498: **NEVER use these patterns:** `batch-fused-*`, `batch-merged-*`, `batch-N-M-*` (range like `batch-8-13.json`), `batches-*`, or any other variant. The downstream merge script (`merge-batch-graphs.py`) requires the regex `batch-(\d+)(?:-part-(\d+))?\.json` — anything else is **silently dropped from the final graph**, losing every node and edge in that file with no error.
499: 
500: **Example.** If your input contained 6 batches (indices 8 through 13), you write EXACTLY 6 output files: `batch-8.json`, `batch-9.json`, `batch-10.json`, `batch-11.json`, `batch-12.json`, `batch-13.json`. Not one combined `batch-fused-8-13.json`. Not one `batch-8-13.json`. Six files, one per original `batchIndex`. Run Steps A–F below independently for each batch's nodes/edges.
501: 
502: **Step A — Compute totals.**
503: ```
504: nodeCount = nodes.length
505: edgeCount = edges.length
506: ```
507: 
508: **Step B — Decide split.**
509: - If `nodeCount ≤ 60` AND `edgeCount ≤ 120`: write ONE file to `$UA_DIR/intermediate/batch-<batchIndex>.json` (the data directory — `.ua/`, or the legacy `.understand-anything/` when present). Done. Skip to Step F.
510: - Otherwise: `parts = ceil(max(nodeCount / 60, edgeCount / 120))`.
511: 
512: **Step C — Partition.**
513: Sort files in your batch alphabetically by path. Chunk them sequentially into `parts` groups of size `ceil(N / parts)`. For each part:
514: - All nodes whose `filePath` is in this part's files (for non-file nodes like `module`/`concept`, use the file they belong to).
515: - All edges whose `source` is in this part's nodes (target may be anywhere — same part, different part of same batch, different batch).
516: 
517: **Step D — Write each part.**
518: Write part `k` (1-indexed) to `$UA_DIR/intermediate/batch-<batchIndex>-part-<k>.json`. Each part is a valid GraphFragment: `{ "nodes": [...], "edges": [...] }`.
519: 
520: **Step E — Self-validate.**
521: For each file written, verify:
522: - Valid JSON.
523: - `nodes` array exists and is well-formed.
524: - For every edge: `source` and `target` both appear as either (a) a node `id` in this part's nodes, OR (b) a `file:<path>` reference where `<path>` is in `neighborMap` or `batchImportData`, OR (c) a `function:<path>:<symbol>` / `class:<path>:<symbol>` reference where `<symbol>` is in some `neighbor.symbols`.
525: 
526: If validation fails on a part, do NOT silently rebuild. Respond with an explicit error stating which part failed, which edge(s) failed validation, and why. The dispatching session can then retry.
527: 
528: **Step F — Respond.**
529: Respond with ONLY a brief text summary: parts written (1 or more), total nodes/edges across all parts, any files skipped. Do NOT include JSON content in the response.
Thinking

Read 1/1 file, 481 lines
1: ---
2: name: architecture-analyzer
3: description: |
4: Analyzes a codebase's file structure, summaries, and import relationships to identify
5: logical architectural layers and assign every file to exactly one layer.
6: ---
7: 
8: # Architecture Analyzer
9: 
10: You are an expert software architect. Your job is to analyze a codebase's file structure, summaries, and import relationships to identify logical architectural layers and assign every file to exactly one layer. Your layer assignments must be well-reasoned and reflect the actual organization of the code, including non-code files like configs, documentation, infrastructure, and data schemas.
11: 
12: ## Task
13: 
14: Given a list of file nodes (with paths, summaries, tags, and node types) and import edges, identify 3-10 logical architecture layers and assign every file node to exactly one layer. You will accomplish this in two phases: first, write and execute a script that computes structural patterns from the import graph and file paths; second, use those structural insights to make semantic layer assignments.
15: 
16: **Language directive:** If the dispatch prompt includes a language directive (e.g., "Generate all textual content in **Chinese**"), apply it to:
17: - Layer `name` — Translate to the specified language (e.g., "API 层", "服务层", "基础设施层")
18: - Layer `description` — Write in the specified language using natural phrasing
19: Use native-level terminology. Keep established English terms when appropriate (e.g., "CI/CD", "ORM", "REST API" may remain untranslated in some languages).
20: 
21: ---
22: 
23: ## Phase 1 -- Structural Analysis Script
24: 
25: Write a script (prefer Node.js; fall back to Python if unavailable) that analyzes the file paths and import edges to compute structural patterns that inform layer identification. The script handles all deterministic graph analysis so you can focus on semantic interpretation.
26: 
27: ### Script Requirements
28: 
29: 1. **Accept** a JSON input file path as the first argument. This file contains:
30: ```json
31: {
32: "fileNodes": [
33: {"id": "file:src/routes/index.ts", "type": "file", "name": "index.ts", "filePath": "src/routes/index.ts", "summary": "...", "tags": ["api-handler"]},
34: {"id": "config:tsconfig.json", "type": "config", "name": "tsconfig.json", "filePath": "tsconfig.json", "summary": "...", "tags": ["configuration"]},
35: {"id": "document:README.md", "type": "document", "name": "README.md", "filePath": "README.md", "summary": "...", "tags": ["documentation"]},
36: {"id": "service:Dockerfile", "type": "service", "name": "Dockerfile", "filePath": "Dockerfile", "summary": "...", "tags": ["infrastructure"]}
37: ],
38: "importEdges": [
39: {"source": "file:src/routes/index.ts", "target": "file:src/services/auth.ts", "type": "imports"}
40: ],
41: "allEdges": [
42: // Only file-level edges (between file-level nodes). Excludes sub-file edges like file→function contains.
43: {"source": "file:src/routes/index.ts", "target": "file:src/services/auth.ts", "type": "imports"},
44: {"source": "config:tsconfig.json", "target": "file:src/index.ts", "type": "configures"},
45: {"source": "service:Dockerfile", "target": "file:src/index.ts", "type": "deploys"}
46: ]
47: }
48: ```
49: 2. **Write** results JSON to the path given as the second argument.
50: 3. **Exit 0** on success. **Exit 1** on fatal error (print error to stderr).
51: 
52: ### What the Script Must Compute
53: 
54: **A. Directory Grouping**
55: 
56: Group all file node IDs by their top-level directory. First, compute the common path prefix shared by all files (e.g., if all paths start with `src/`, the common prefix is `src/`). Then group by the first directory segment after that prefix. For example, with prefix `src/`:
57: - `src/routes/index.ts` -> group `routes`
58: - `src/services/auth.ts` -> group `services`
59: - `src/utils/format.ts` -> group `utils`
60: 
61: If files have no common prefix (e.g., `src/foo.ts`, `lib/bar.ts`, `config.json`), group by their first directory segment (`src`, `lib`, root).
62: 
63: If the project has a flat structure (all files in one directory with no subdirectories), group by file type/extension pattern (e.g., `*.test.ts` → `test`, `*.config.*` → `config`).
64: 
65: **B. Node Type Grouping**
66: 
67: Group all file node IDs by their node type (`file`, `config`, `document`, `service`, `pipeline`, `table`, `schema`, `resource`, `endpoint`). This reveals the distribution of code vs. non-code files.
68: 
69: **C. Import Adjacency Matrix**
70: 
71: Build an adjacency list of which files import which other files. Compute:
72: - For each file: fan-out (how many files it imports) and fan-in (how many files import it)
73: - For each directory group: the set of other groups it imports from and is imported by
74: 
75: **D. Cross-Category Dependency Analysis**
76: 
77: Using `allEdges`, compute cross-category relationships:
78: - Count edges of each type between node type groups (e.g., config→file configures edges, service→file deploys edges)
79: - Identify which non-code nodes connect to which code nodes
80: - Output a matrix:
81: ```
82: config -> file: 5 (configures)
83: document -> file: 3 (documents)
84: service -> file: 2 (deploys)
85: pipeline -> file: 1 (triggers)
86: schema -> file: 2 (defines_schema)
87: ```
88: 
89: **E. Inter-Group Import Frequency**
90: 
91: For every pair of directory groups, count the number of import edges between them. Produce a matrix:
92: ```
93: routes -> services: 12
94: routes -> utils: 3
95: services -> models: 8
96: services -> utils: 5
97: ```
98: 
99: This reveals dependency direction between groups.
100: 
101: **F. Intra-Group Import Density**
102: 
103: For each directory group, count how many import edges exist between files within the same group versus total edges involving that group. High intra-group density suggests the group is cohesive and should be its own layer.
104: 
105: **G. Directory Pattern Matching**
106: 
107: Classify each directory name against known architectural patterns:
108: 
109: | Directory Patterns | Pattern Label |
110: |---|---|
111: | `routes`, `api`, `controllers`, `endpoints`, `handlers` | `api` |
112: | `services`, `core`, `lib`, `domain`, `logic` | `service` |
113: | `models`, `db`, `data`, `persistence`, `repository`, `entities` | `data` |
114: | `components`, `views`, `pages`, `ui`, `layouts`, `screens` | `ui` |
115: | `middleware`, `plugins`, `interceptors`, `guards` | `middleware` |
116: | `utils`, `helpers`, `common`, `shared`, `tools` | `utility` |
117: | `config`, `constants`, `env`, `settings` | `config` |
118: | `__tests__`, `test`, `tests`, `spec`, `specs` | `test` |
119: | `types`, `interfaces`, `schemas`, `contracts`, `dtos` | `types` |
120: | `hooks` | `hooks` |
121: | `store`, `state`, `reducers`, `actions`, `slices` | `state` |
122: | `assets`, `static`, `public` | `assets` |
123: | `migrations` | `data` |
124: | `management`, `commands` | `config` |
125: | `templatetags` | `utility` |
126: | `signals` | `service` |
127: | `serializers` | `api` |
128: | `cmd` | `entry` |
129: | `internal` | `service` |
130: | `pkg` | `utility` |
131: | `src/main/java` | `service` |
132: | `src/test/java` | `test` |
133: | `dto`, `request`, `response` | `types` |
134: | `entity` | `data` |
135: | `controller` | `api` |
136: | `routers` | `api` |
137: | `composables` | `service` |
138: | `blueprints` | `api` |
139: | `mailers`, `jobs`, `channels` | `service` |
140: | `bin` | `entry` |
141: | `docs`, `documentation`, `wiki` | `documentation` |
142: | `deploy`, `deployment`, `infra`, `infrastructure` | `infrastructure` |
143: | `.github`, `.gitlab`, `.circleci` | `ci-cd` |
144: | `k8s`, `kubernetes`, `helm`, `charts` | `infrastructure` |
145: | `terraform`, `tf` | `infrastructure` |
146: | `docker` | `infrastructure` |
147: | `sql`, `database`, `schema` | `data` |
148: 
149: Also check file-level patterns:
150: - Files matching `*.test.*` or `*.spec.*` or `test_*.py` or `*_test.go` or `*Test.java` or `*_spec.rb` or `*Test.php` or `*Tests.cs` -> `test`
151: - Files matching `*.d.ts` -> `types` (TypeScript declaration files only)
152: - Files named `index.ts`, `index.js`, or `__init__.py` at a package/directory root -> `entry`
153: - Files named `manage.py` at the project root -> `entry` (Django management entry point)
154: - Files named `wsgi.py` or `asgi.py` -> `config` (Python WSGI/ASGI server config)
155: - Files named `main.go` at `cmd/*/` -> `entry` (Go binary entry points)
156: - Files named `main.rs` or `lib.rs` at `src/` -> `entry` (Rust crate roots)
157: - Files named `Application.java` or `Program.cs` -> `entry` (JVM / .NET entry points)
158: - Files named `config.ru` -> `entry` (Ruby Rack entry point)
159: - Files named `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`, `Gemfile`, `pom.xml`, `build.gradle`, `composer.json` -> `config` (language-level project config)
160: - `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.*` -> `infrastructure`
161: - `*.tf`, `*.tfvars` -> `infrastructure`
162: - `.github/workflows/*`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Jenkinsfile` -> `ci-cd`
163: - `*.sql` -> `data`
164: - `*.graphql`, `*.gql`, `*.proto` -> `types`
165: - `*.md`, `*.rst` -> `documentation`
166: - `Makefile` -> `infrastructure`
167: 
168: **H. Deployment Topology Detection**
169: 
170: Identify deployment-related files and their relationships:
171: - Look for Dockerfile → docker-compose → K8s manifests chains
172: - Detect multi-environment configurations (e.g., Dockerfile.dev, Dockerfile.prod, docker-compose.prod.yml)
173: - Identify infrastructure-as-code layering (Terraform modules, CloudFormation stacks)
174: 
175: Output:
176: ```json
177: "deploymentTopology": {
178: "hasDockerfile": true,
179: "hasCompose": true,
180: "hasK8s": false,
181: "hasTerraform": false,
182: "hasCI": true,
183: "infraFiles": ["Dockerfile", "docker-compose.yml", ".github/workflows/ci.yml"]
184: }
185: ```
186: 
187: **I. Data Pipeline Detection**
188: 
189: Identify data flow patterns:
190: - Schema definition files → migration files → API endpoint handlers → client code
191: - Database schemas → ORM models → service layer → API layer
192: - Protobuf/GraphQL definitions → generated code → service handlers
193: 
194: Output:
195: ```json
196: "dataPipeline": {
197: "schemaFiles": ["schema.sql", "schema.graphql"],
198: "migrationFiles": ["migrations/001_init.sql"],
199: "dataModelFiles": ["src/models/user.ts"],
200: "apiHandlerFiles": ["src/routes/users.ts"]
201: }
202: ```
203: 
204: **J. Documentation Coverage**
205: 
206: For each directory group, check if there are documentation files:
207: - Does the directory have a README.md?
208: - Are there docs/*.md files that reference code in this group?
209: - Calculate a coverage ratio: groups-with-docs / total-groups
210: 
211: Output:
212: ```json
213: "docCoverage": {
214: "groupsWithDocs": 3,
215: "totalGroups": 7,
216: "coverageRatio": 0.43,
217: "undocumentedGroups": ["middleware", "utils", "state", "types"]
218: }
219: ```
220: 
221: **K. Dependency Direction**
222: 
223: For each pair of groups with imports between them, determine the dominant direction. If group A imports from group B more than B imports from A, then A depends on B. Output this as a list of directed dependency relationships.
224:

325: 
326: ## Phase 2 -- Semantic Layer Assignment
327: 
328: After the script completes, read `$UA_DIR/tmp/ua-arch-results.json`. Use the structural analysis as the primary input for your layer decisions. Do NOT re-read source files or re-analyze imports -- trust the script's results entirely.
329: 
330: ### Step 1 -- Evaluate Directory Groups as Layer Candidates
331: 
332: For each directory group from the script output:
333: 
334: 1. Check if `patternMatches` assigned it a known pattern label. If yes, this is a strong signal for what layer it belongs to.
335: 2. Check `intraGroupDensity`. High density (>0.3) suggests the group is cohesive and should likely be its own layer.
336: 3. Check `interGroupImports`. Groups that are heavily imported by others but import few groups themselves are likely foundational layers (utility, types, data).
337: 
338: ### Step 2 -- Analyze Dependency Direction
339: 
340: Use the `dependencyDirection` data to understand the project's layering:
341: - Top-level layers (API, UI) depend on middle layers (Service, State)
342: - Middle layers depend on bottom layers (Data, Utility, Types)
343: - This forms a dependency hierarchy that should map to your layer ordering
344: 
345: ### Step 3 -- Consider Non-Code Layers
346: 
347: Use `nodeTypeGroups` and `deploymentTopology` to determine if non-code layers are warranted:
348: 
349: - **Infrastructure layer:** Create if the project has Dockerfiles, Terraform, K8s manifests, or other deployment files. Include all `service` and `resource` type nodes.
350: - **CI/CD layer:** Create if the project has CI/CD configs (.github/workflows, .gitlab-ci.yml, Jenkinsfile). Include all `pipeline` type nodes. May be merged with Infrastructure if few files.
351: - **Documentation layer:** Create if the project has 3+ documentation files (README, guides, API docs). Include all `document` type nodes. May be merged with a "Project" or "Root" layer if few files.
352: - **Data layer:** Create if the project has SQL, GraphQL, Protobuf, or other schema files. Include `table`, `schema`, and `endpoint` type nodes. May be merged with an existing "Data" or "Models" layer.
353: - **Configuration layer:** Create if the project has 3+ config files beyond just package.json. Include all `config` type nodes. May be merged with a "Root" or "Project" layer if few files.
354: 
355: **Merging guidance:** For small projects, merge non-code layers into a single "Project Support" or "Infrastructure & Config" layer rather than creating many single-file layers. For larger projects, separate them into distinct layers.
356: 
357: ### Step 4 -- Consider File Summaries and Tags
358: 
359: When directory structure alone is ambiguous (e.g., a flat `src/` directory with no subdirectories), use the file summaries and tags from the input data to determine each file's role. Think about what responsibility the file fulfills in the system.
360: 
361: ### Step 5 -- Select 3-10 Layers
362: 
363: Choose layers based on the project's actual architecture, informed by the script's structural data. Common patterns include:
364: - **Layered architecture:** API -> Service -> Data + Infrastructure + Config
365: - **Component-based:** UI Components, State, Services, Utils, Infrastructure
366: - **MVC:** Models, Views, Controllers + Config + Docs
367: - **Monorepo packages:** Each package forms its own layer + shared infra
368: - **Library:** Core, Plugins, Types, Tests, Documentation
369: 
370: **Layer hint for non-code files:**
371: 
372: | Pattern | Suggested Layer |
373: |---|---|
374: | Dockerfile, docker-compose.*, K8s manifests, Terraform | `layer:infrastructure` |
375: | .github/workflows/*, .gitlab-ci.yml, Jenkinsfile | `layer:ci-cd` or merge into `layer:infrastructure` |
376: | README.md, docs/*.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, CHANGELOG.md | `layer:documentation` or merge into relevant code layer |
377: | *.sql, migrations/*.sql | `layer:data` |
378: | *.graphql, *.proto, *.prisma | `layer:data` or `layer:types` |
379: | package.json, tsconfig.json, *.toml, *.yaml configs | `layer:config` or merge into relevant code layer |
380: 
381: Merge small directory groups into larger layers when they share a common purpose. Prefer fewer, well-defined layers over many granular ones.
382: 
383: ### Step 6 -- Assign Every File Node
384: 
385: Go through each file node ID from the input and assign it to exactly one layer. Use the `directoryGroups` mapping as the primary assignment mechanism -- most files in the same directory group should end up in the same layer.
386: 
387: For non-code files, use the node type as the primary signal:
388: - `config` nodes → Configuration or root layer
389: - `document` nodes → Documentation layer
390: - `service`, `resource` nodes → Infrastructure layer
391: - `pipeline` nodes → CI/CD or Infrastructure layer
392: - `table`, `schema`, `endpoint` nodes → Data layer
393: 
394: For files that do not clearly fit any layer, place them in the most relevant layer or create a "Shared" / "Utility" catch-all layer. Do not leave any file unassigned.
395: 
396: **Cross-check:** The sum of all `nodeIds` array lengths across all layers MUST equal the total number of file nodes from the input (`fileStats.totalFileNodes` from the script output).
397: 
398: ## Layer ID Format
399: 
400: Use `layer:<kebab-case>` format consistently:
401: - `layer:api`, `layer:service`, `layer:data`, `layer:ui`, `layer:middleware`
402: - `layer:utility`, `layer:config`, `layer:test`, `layer:types`, `layer:state`
403: - `layer:infrastructure`, `layer:documentation`, `layer:ci-cd`
404: 
405: ## Output Format
406: 
407: Produce a single, valid JSON array. Every field shown is **required**.
408: 
409: ```json
410: [
411: {
412: "id": "layer:api",
413: "name": "API Layer",
414: "description": "HTTP endpoints, route handlers, and request/response processing",
415: "nodeIds": ["file:src/routes/index.ts", "file:src/controllers/auth.ts"]
416: },
417: {
418: "id": "layer:service",
419: "name": "Service Layer",
420: "description": "Core business logic, domain services, and orchestration",
421: "nodeIds": ["file:src/services/auth.ts", "file:src/services/user.ts"]

456: **Required fields for every layer:**
457: - `id` (string) -- must follow `layer:<kebab-case>` format
458: - `name` (string) -- human-readable name, title-cased
459: - `description` (string) -- 1 sentence describing the layer's responsibility, specific to this project (not generic boilerplate)
460: - `nodeIds` (string[]) -- non-empty array of file node IDs belonging to this layer
461: 
462: ## Critical Constraints
463: 
464: - EVERY file node ID from the input MUST appear in exactly one layer's `nodeIds` array. Missing file assignments break the downstream pipeline. This includes non-code nodes (config, document, service, pipeline, table, schema, resource, endpoint).
465: - NEVER include node IDs in `nodeIds` that were not provided in the input. Do not invent node IDs.
466: - NEVER create a layer with an empty `nodeIds` array.
467: - ALWAYS verify your output accounts for all input file nodes. Count them: the sum of all `nodeIds` array lengths must equal the total number of input file nodes.
468: - Keep to 3-10 layers. If the project is very small (under 10 files), 3 layers is sufficient. If large (100+ files), up to 10 is appropriate. Before writing output, count your layers and verify the count is within this range.
469: - Layer `description` must be specific to this project, not generic boilerplate.
470: - Trust the script's structural analysis. Do NOT re-read source files or re-count imports. The script's adjacency data, density calculations, and pattern matches are deterministic and reliable.
471: - If the script produces empty directory groups or groups with zero files, skip them — do not create empty layers.

478: 2. The project root will be provided in your prompt.

POWERSHELL
$ua = "C:/Users/Ivermectin/Desktop/NLP/NLP/.ua"
$script = @'
#!/usr/bin/env node
const fs = require('fs');
const graphPath = process.argv[2];
const outputPath = process.argv[3];
try {
 const graph = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(graphPath, 'utf8'));
 const issues = [], warnings = [];
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.nodes)) { issues.push('graph.nodes is missing or not an array'); graph.nodes = []; }
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.edges)) { issues.push('graph.edges is missing or not an array'); graph.edges = []; }
 const nodeIds = new Set();
 const seen = new Map();
 graph.nodes.forEach((n, i) => {
 if (!n.id) { issues.push(`Node[${i}] missing id`); return; }
 if (!n.type) issues.push(`Node[${i}] '${n.id}' missing type`);
 if (!n.name) issues.push(`Node[${i}] '${n.id}' missing name`);
 if (!n.summary) issues.push(`Node[${i}] '${n.id}' missing summary`);
 if (!n.tags || !n.tags.length) issues.push(`Node[${i}] '${n.id}' missing tags`);
 if (seen.has(n.id)) issues.push(`Duplicate node ID '${n.id}' at indices ${seen.get(n.id)} and ${i}`);
 else seen.set(n.id, i);
 nodeIds.add(n.id);
 });
 graph.edges.forEach((e, i) => {
 if (!nodeIds.has(e.source)) issues.push(`Edge[${i}] source '${e.source}' not found`);
 if (!nodeIds.has(e.target)) issues.push(`Edge[${i}] target '${e.target}' not found`);
 });
 const fileLevelTypes = new Set(['file', 'config', 'document', 'service', 'pipeline', 'table', 'schema', 'resource', 'endpoint']);
 const fileNodes = graph.nodes.filter(n => fileLevelTypes.has(n.type)).map(n => n.id);
 const assigned = new Map();
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.layers)) { if (graph.layers) warnings.push('graph.layers is not an array'); graph.layers = []; }
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.tour)) { if (graph.tour) warnings.push('graph.tour is not an array'); graph.tour = []; }
 graph.layers.forEach(layer => {
 (layer.nodeIds || []).forEach(id => {
 if (!nodeIds.has(id)) issues.push(`Layer '${layer.id}' refs missing node '${id}'`);
 if (assigned.has(id)) issues.push(`Node '${id}' appears in multiple layers`);
 assigned.set(id, layer.id);
 });
 });
 fileNodes.forEach(id => {
 if (!assigned.has(id)) issues.push(`File node '${id}' not in any layer`);
 });
 graph.tour.forEach((step, i) => {
 (step.nodeIds || []).forEach(id => {
 if (!nodeIds.has(id)) issues.push(`Tour step[${i}] refs missing node '${id}'`);
 });
 });
 const withEdges = new Set([
 ...graph.edges.map(e => e.source),
 ...graph.edges.map(e => e.target)
 ]);
 graph.nodes.forEach(n => {
 if (!withEdges.has(n.id)) warnings.push(`Node '${n.id}' has no edges (orphan)`);
 });
 const stats = {
 totalNodes: graph.nodes.length,
 totalEdges: graph.edges.length,
 totalLayers: graph.layers.length,
 tourSteps: graph.tour.length,
 nodeTypes: graph.nodes.reduce((a, n) => { a[n.type] = (a[n.type]||0)+1; return a; }, {}),
 edgeTypes: graph.edges.reduce((a, e) => { a[e.type] = (a[e.type]||0)+1; return a; }, {})
 };
 fs.writeFileSync(outputPath, JSON.stringify({ issues, warnings, stats }, null, 2));
 process.exit(0);
} catch (err) { process.stderr.write(err.message + '\n'); process.exit(1); }
'@
$script | Out-File -FilePath "$ua/tmp/ua-inline-validate.cjs" -Encoding utf8
node "$ua/tmp/ua-inline-validate.cjs" "$ua/intermediate/assembled-graph.json" "$ua/intermediate/review.json"
Write-Host "exit=$LASTEXITCODE"

" <<'PYEOF'
#!/usr/bin/env node
const fs = require('fs');
const graphPath = process.argv[2];
const outputPath = process.argv[3];
try {
 const graph = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(graphPath, 'utf8'));
 const issues = [], warnings = [];
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.nodes)) { issues.push('graph.nodes is missing or not an array'); graph.nodes = []; }
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.edges)) { issues.push('graph.edges is missing or not an array'); graph.edges = []; }
 const nodeIds = new Set();
 const seen = new Map();
 graph.nodes.forEach((n, i) => {
 if (!n.id) { issues.push('Node['+i+'] missing id'); return; }
 if (!n.type) issues.push('Node['+i+'] '+n.id+' missing type');
 if (!n.name) issues.push('Node['+i+'] '+n.id+' missing name');
 if (!n.summary) issues.push('Node['+i+'] '+n.id+' missing summary');
 if (!n.tags || !n.tags.length) issues.push('Node['+i+'] '+n.id+' missing tags');
 if (seen.has(n.id)) issues.push('Duplicate node ID '+n.id+' at indices '+seen.get(n.id)+' and '+i);
 else seen.set(n.id, i);
 nodeIds.add(n.id);
 });
 graph.edges.forEach((e, i) => {
 if (!nodeIds.has(e.source)) issues.push('Edge['+i+'] source '+e.source+' not found');
 if (!nodeIds.has(e.target)) issues.push('Edge['+i+'] target '+e.target+' not found');
 });
 const fileLevelTypes = new Set(['file', 'config', 'document', 'service', 'pipeline', 'table', 'schema', 'resource', 'endpoint']);
 const fileNodes = graph.nodes.filter(n => fileLevelTypes.has(n.type)).map(n => n.id);
 const assigned = new Map();
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.layers)) { if (graph.layers) warnings.push('graph.layers is not an array'); graph.layers = []; }
 if (!Array.isArray(graph.tour)) { if (graph.tour) warnings.push('graph.tour is not an array'); graph.tour = []; }
 graph.layers.forEach(layer => {
 (layer.nodeIds || []).forEach(id => {
 if (!nodeIds.has(id)) issues.push('Layer '+layer.id+' refs missing node '+id);
 if (assigned.has(id)) issues.push('Node '+id+' appears in multiple layers');
 assigned.set(id, layer.id);
 });
 });
 fileNodes.forEach(id => {
 if (!assigned.has(id)) issues.push('File node '+id+' not in any layer');
 });
 graph.tour.forEach((step, i) => {
 (step.nodeIds || []).forEach(id => {
 if (!nodeIds.has(id)) issues.push('Tour step['+i+'] refs missing node '+id);
 });
 });
 const withEdges = new Set([...graph.edges.map(e => e.source), ...graph.edges.map(e => e.target)]);
 graph.nodes.forEach(n => {
 if (!withEdges.has(n.id)) warnings.push('Node '+n.id+' has no edges (orphan)');
 });
 const stats = {
 totalNodes: graph.nodes.length,
 totalEdges: graph.edges.length,
 totalLayers: graph.layers.length,
 tourSteps: graph.tour.length,
 nodeTypes: graph.nodes.reduce((a, n) => { a[n.type] = (a[n.type]||0)+1; return a; }, {}),
 edgeTypes: graph.edges.reduce((a, e) => { a[e.type] = (a[e.type]||0)+1; return a; }, {})
 };
 fs.writeFileSync(outputPath, JSON.stringify({ issues, warnings, stats }, null, 2));
 process.exit(0);
} catch (err) { process.stderr.write(err.message + '\n'); process.exit(1); }
PYEOF
