$ua = "C:/Users/Ivermectin/Desktop/NLP/NLP/.ua"
$py = @"
import json
graph = json.load(open('$ua/intermediate/assembled-graph.json'))
issues = []
warnings = []
if not isinstance(graph.get('nodes'), list):
 issues.append('graph.nodes missing/not array'); graph['nodes'] = []
if not isinstance(graph.get('edges'), list):
 issues.append('graph.edges missing/not array'); graph['edges'] = []
node_ids = set()
seen = {}
for i, n in enumerate(graph['nodes']):
 if not n.get('id'):
 issues.append(f'Node[{i}] missing id'); continue
 if not n.get('type'): issues.append(f\"Node[{i}] '{n['id']}' missing type\")
 if not n.get('name'): issues.append(f\"Node[{i}] '{n['id']}' missing name\")
 if not n.get('summary'): issues.append(f\"Node[{i}] '{n['id']}' missing summary\")
 if not n.get('tags'): issues.append(f\"Node[{i}] '{n['id']}' missing tags\")
 if n['id'] in seen: issues.append(f\"Duplicate node ID '{n['id']}' at {seen[n['id']]} and {i}\")
 else: seen[n['id']] = i
 node_ids.add(n['id'])
for i, e in enumerate(graph['edges']):
 if e['source'] not in node_ids: issues.append(f\"Edge[{i}] source '{e['source']}' not found\")
 if e['target'] not in node_ids: issues.append(f\"Edge[{i}] target '{e['target']}' not found\")
file_level_types = {'file','config','document','service','pipeline','table','schema','resource','endpoint'}
file_nodes = [n['id'] for n in graph['nodes'] if n.get('type') in file_level_types]
assigned = {}
for layer in (graph.get('layers') or []):
 for nid in (layer.get('nodeIds') or []):
 if nid not in node_ids: issues.append(f\"Layer '{layer.get('id')}' refs missing node '{nid}'\")
 if nid in assigned: issues.append(f\"Node '{nid}' appears in multiple layers\")
 assigned[nid] = layer.get('id')
for nid in file_nodes:
 if nid not in assigned: issues.append(f\"File node '{nid}' not in any layer\")
for i, step in enumerate(graph.get('tour') or []):
 for nid in (step.get('nodeIds') or []):
 if nid not in node_ids: issues.append(f\"Tour step[{i}] refs missing node '{nid}'\")
with_edges = set()
for e in graph['edges']:
 with_edges.add(e['source']); with_edges.add(e['target'])
for n in graph['nodes']:
 if n['id'] not in with_edges: warnings.append(f\"Node '{n['id']}' has no edges (orphan)\")
stats = {
 'totalNodes': len(graph['nodes']),
 'totalEdges': len(graph['edges']),
 'totalLayers': len(graph.get('layers') or []),
 'tourSteps': len(graph.get('tour') or []),
 'nodeTypes': {},
 'edgeTypes': {}
}
for n in graph['nodes']:
 stats['nodeTypes'][n.get('type')] = stats['nodeTypes'].get(n.get('type'),0)+1
for e in graph['edges']:
 stats['edgeTypes'][e.get('type')] = stats['edgeTypes'].get(e.get('type'),0)+1
json.dump({'issues': issues, 'warnings': warnings, 'stats': stats}, open('$ua/intermediate/review.json','w'), indent=2)
print('issues:', len(issues))
print('warnings:', len(warnings))
print(json.dumps(stats, indent=2))

layer:api | API Layer | FastAPI route handlers, dependency injection, and HTTP entry point for contract analysis endpoints. | files: 7
layer:ui | UI Layer | Streamlit multi-page dashboard for summary, clause exploration, obligations, graph, and risk review. | files: 9

=== TOUR ===
1 | Project Overview | Start with README.md to understand BharatContract-AI's purpose: Indian-context legal contract intelligence for risk-aware clause extraction and obligation tracking. It outlines the architecture, quick start, environment variables, auth, ML classifier, and tests, giving a roadmap before diving into code.
 nodes: ['document:README.md']
2 | Application Entry Point | app/main.py bootstraps the FastAPI service: it wires routers, middleware, exception handlers, and the lifespan that initializes logging and database connections. This file gives a bird's-eye view of the runtime structure and how requests enter the system.
 nodes: ['file:app/main.py']
3 | Core Models and Config | app/models/schemas.py (high fan-in) defines the shared domain vocabulary—contracts, clauses, obligations, risks—used across API, services, and UI. app/core/config.py centralizes settings from environment variables, including database URLs, model paths, and auth toggles.
 nodes: ['file:app/models/schemas.py', 'file:app/core/config.py']
4 | API Layer and Auth | The API layer exposes contract upload, summary, clause, obligation, risk, and graph endpoints, plus auth login/me and job status routes. Dependency injection in app/api/deps.py resolves the current principal and enforces role-based access control.
 nodes: ['file:app/api/routes_contracts.py', 'file:app/api/deps.py', 'file:app/api/routes_auth.py']
5 | Service Pipeline | app/services/analyzer.py orchestrates the end-to-end analysis: parsing uploads, segmenting text into clauses, classifying them, extracting obligations, and scoring risks. It coordinates parser, segmenter, and NLP components before persisting results.
 nodes: ['file:app/services/analyzer.py', 'file:app/services/parser.py', 'file:app/services/segmenter.py']
6 | Clause Classification | nlp/clause_classification/classifier.py implements a rule-based keyword matcher enriched by a CUAD-derived keyword bank, with a TF-IDF ML fallback via tfidf_model.py and an optional DeBERTa-v3 path in classifier_ml.py. It exposes classify_clause and classify_clauses returning typed results.
 nodes: ['file:nlp/clause_classification/classifier.py', 'file:nlp/clause_classification/tfidf_model.py', 'file:nlp/clause_classification/classifier_ml.py']
7 | NER and Obligation Extraction | nlp/ner/ner.py extracts entities such as parties, dates, and monetary amounts, while nlp/obligation_extraction/extractor.py turns clause text into actionable obligations with owners and deadlines. These feed the obligation tracker surfaced in the UI.
 nodes: ['file:nlp/ner/ner.py', 'file:nlp/obligation_extraction/extractor.py']
8 | Risk Scoring | nlp/risk_scoring/scorer.py aggregates rule-based flags from rules.py and case-law signals from case_law.py into a contract risk profile. risk_model.py supports the learned risk classifier, with evidence binding tying each flag back to source clause text.
 nodes: ['file:nlp/risk_scoring/scorer.py', 'file:nlp/risk_scoring/rules.py', 'file:nlp/risk_scoring/risk_model.py']
9 | Context Resolution and Taxonomy | nlp/context/resolver.py resolves cross-references and contextual meaning across clauses, while nlp/taxonomy.py defines the clause/obligation taxonomy. Together they improve extraction accuracy on long, multi-clause contracts.
 nodes: ['file:nlp/context/resolver.py', 'file:nlp/taxonomy.py']
10 | Data and Graph Persistence | app/db/postgres.py manages the relational store for contracts and jobs, app/db/queries.py upserts clauses/obligations/risks into Neo4j, and neo4j.py provides the graph session and indexes. The graph powers the clause/obligation relationship view.
 nodes: ['file:app/db/postgres.py', 'file:app/db/queries.py', 'file:app/db/neo4j.py']
11 | Streamlit Dashboard | streamlit_app/Home.py and api_client.py connect the UI to the backend, while pages for summary, clause explorer, obligations, graph, and risk review present analysis results. Risk cards render flagged issues for reviewer triage.
 nodes: ['file:streamlit_app/Home.py', 'file:streamlit_app/api_client.py', 'file:streamlit_app/components/risk_card.py']
12 | Containerization and Deployment | The Dockerfile packages the app into a production image, and docker-compose.yml orchestrates Postgres, Neo4j, Redis, the API, and Streamlit services. This shows how the full stack is deployed and scaled.
 nodes: ['service:docker/Dockerfile', 'service:docker/docker-compose.yml']
13 | Dataset Provenance and Training | nlp/datasets and nlp/provenance.py manage CUAD/MAUD loading, splits, manifests, and model provenance, while scripts build training data and train classifiers. This underpins the ML components and ensures reproducible evaluation.
 nodes: ['file:nlp/datasets/cuad.py', 'file:nlp/provenance.py', 'file:scripts/build_training_data.py']

/arbor # BharatContract AI — 8-Hour Autonomous Engineering & Research Integrity Mission
## Mission
You are operating in the existing BharatContract AI repository using the Arbor skill and the Beam 1.0 model.
Your task is to make the project measurably more correct, reliable, testable, and defensible as a college research project. Work autonomously for up to 8 hours, with an absolute maximum of 10 hours, subject to the runtime and tool limits of the environment.
Do not spend time merely generating documentation, repeatedly inspecting the same files, or making cosmetic changes. Prioritize demonstrable correctness improvements, independent tests, failure analysis, and reproducible evidence.
**The objective is verified engineering progress, not maximum lines of code or maximum number of completed tasks.**
## 1. Mandatory first action: recover the project state
Before changing anything, read these files in order:
1. `.commandcode/BHARATCONTRACT_PROJECT_MEMORY.md`
2. `.commandcode/BHARATCONTRACT_NEXT_SESSION.md`
3. `.commandcode/BHARATCONTRACT_DECISION_LOG.md`
4. `.commandcode/BHARATCONTRACT_ARTIFACT_INDEX.md`
5. `.commandcode/BHARATCONTRACT_SESSION_ARCHIVE.md`
Then inspect the current repository state:
- Current directory, branch, HEAD commit, and upstream status.
- `git status --short --untracked-files=all`
- Existing tracked diffs and untracked files.
- Relevant current reports in `reports/evaluations/`.
- The existing P1 context resolver and P2 evidence-binding implementation.
- Relevant tests and application schemas.
- The seven outstanding provenance-related test failures.
The handoff describes earlier observations. Treat them as leads to verify, not unquestionable facts.
### Protect existing work
The repository already contains substantial uncommitted work.
- Do not discard, reset, clean, stash, overwrite, or reformat existing work indiscriminately.
- Do not assume untracked files are disposable.
- Do not overwrite the five newly exported `.commandcode/BHARATCONTRACT_*.md` files.
- Do not modify unrelated existing work merely to simplify your task.
- Record the initial Git status and HEAD in your task report.
- Distinguish pre-existing changes from changes introduced during this mission.
- Do not commit, push, amend, rebase, or rewrite Git history.
If a proposed change intersects existing modifications, inspect the diff and preserve all existing intent before proceeding.
## 2. Non-negotiable integrity rules
These rules override task completion targets.
### No unsupported claims
- Never claim a test passed unless you ran it and inspected its exit status and result.
- Never claim a defect is fixed without a regression test or another appropriate verification.
- Never report a benchmark improvement without an actual, reproducible comparison.
- Never infer Indian-law compliance from US-centric CUAD or MAUD datasets.
- Never invent legal authorities, quotations, holdings, source URLs, or legal interpretations.
- Clearly distinguish observed facts, agent-reported facts, hypotheses, and unverified assumptions.
- When you cannot verify something, record it as unverified.
### Preserve provenance and evaluation integrity
The existing provenance-related test failures are important and must remain visible.
Previously reported full-suite state:
- 81 tests collected.
- 74 passed.
- 7 failed, involving missing or inadequate provenance manifests for existing production artifacts.
These counts are historical until independently reproduced.
Do not:
- Delete or skip these tests.
- Weaken assertions to make the suite green.
- Add broad exception handlers to conceal failures.
- Create fake manifests or fabricate lineage.
- Label unverified artifacts as validated.
- Swap staged artifacts into production.
- Retrain models on held-out data.
- Treat text overlap alone as proof of leakage.
A legitimate fix is allowed only if it establishes the missing property using truthful, verifiable evidence. Otherwise, document the blocker and preserve the failure.
### Preserve the research baseline
Do not modify, regenerate, replace, delete, or extract:
- `data/datasets/`
- `data/processed/`
- `data/processed.staging/`
- Existing model weights, archives, or caches.
- Production model artifacts.
- Existing experiment results or benchmark outputs.
Do not run large training jobs, download large datasets, or introduce paid services.
No production artifact swap or model retraining is authorized.
## 3. Time budget and execution discipline
Use a maximum of 8 hours for productive work. You may continue up to 10 hours only if the environment permits and there is a concrete, safe, high-value task remaining.
Do not attempt to keep the process alive artificially. Stop early if remaining tasks require unavailable evidence, infrastructure, or approvals.
Use these phases as a planning guide, not as an excuse to fill time:
### Phase A — State recovery and baseline (first 30–60 minutes)
- Read the handoff and inspect the repository.
- Reproduce the relevant baseline checks.
- Run the focused P1 and P2 tests.
- Run the full test suite once if practical.
- Record actual failures, warnings, skipped tests, and exit codes.
- Inspect existing changes before deciding what to edit.
### Phase B — Root-cause investigation (approximately 1–2 hours)
Investigate the highest-impact correctness gaps, prioritizing:
1. Whole-contract context and cross-reference handling.
2. Evidence quality and traceability of risk findings.
3. False positives, unsupported claims, and uncertainty handling.
4. Obligation extraction correctness.
5. Missing-protection and internal-inconsistency detection.
6. Provenance and reproducibility safeguards.
Read the actual implementations and tests before proposing fixes.
Create a short ranked task list with:
- Observed failure.
- Reproduction steps.
- Root cause or explicitly unresolved hypothesis.
- Expected benefit.
- Risk of regression.
- Verification plan.
### Phase C — Targeted engineering improvements (approximately 2–4 hours)
Select the highest-value, lowest-risk improvements that can be validated within the available time.
Implement only changes supported by the code, tests, and observed behavior.
Prefer:
- Small deterministic fixes.
- Regression tests for genuine defects.
- Clear evidence and uncertainty semantics.
- Correct handling of missing or ambiguous references.
- Improved detection of false positives.
- Better handling of clause IDs, offsets, and document-level findings.
- Reproducible tests using synthetic contracts.
- Explicit abstention when the system lacks enough evidence.
Avoid broad architectural changes.
### Phase D — Independent adversarial validation (approximately 1–2 hours)
Test the implementation against more than the exact example used to develop it.
Build or reuse synthetic contract fixtures covering:
- Definitions referenced elsewhere in the contract.
- Cross-references and precedence clauses.
- Exceptions and carve-outs.
- Conflicting provisions.
- Schedule overrides.
- Explicitly present protections.
- Genuinely missing protections.
- Ambiguous or unresolved references.
- Unrelated keywords that could trigger false positives.
- Long clauses and segmented text.
- Contract-level findings without a defensible clause locator.
- Irrelevant or nonsensical input.
- Uncertain legal interpretations.
Do not hard-code behavior specifically to one named fixture.
Where practical, include positive, negative, and adversarial cases. Ensure tests check both expected findings and findings that should not be produced.
Use the existing TC-05 cross-reference diagnostic as one diagnostic fixture, not as the sole evaluation target.
Do not tune to a held-out benchmark and then present that same benchmark as independent validation.
### Phase E — Final verification and handoff (final 1–2 hours)
- Run focused tests for each changed area.
- Run Ruff or the project's existing lint checks.
- Run the full suite if practical.
- Verify the final diff and Git status.
- Review new tests for false confidence, brittle assumptions, or assertions that merely restate the implementation.
- Write a report of what was actually accomplished.
- Identify the next highest-value task rather than starting an unrelated expansion.
## 4. Specific engineering priorities
### Priority 1: Context-aware contract analysis
Inspect `nlp/context/resolver.py`, its tests, and the integration in `app/services/analyzer.py`.
The existing resolver is reported to produce reference statuses of `resolved`, `missing`, and `ambiguous`, along with references, overrides, related clause IDs, and unresolved references.
Verify:
- Whether context is actually consumed by downstream risk rules.
- Whether section and schedule references resolve correctly.
- Whether unresolved references remain unresolved rather than being silently guessed.
- Whether candidate precedence is mistakenly treated as proof of a legal conflict being resolved.
- Whether headings, numbering variants, and schedule references cause missed or incorrect associations.
Improve downstream behavior only if you can define reliable semantics and prove them through tests.
Do not claim that the resolver improves legal accuracy merely because it returns metadata.
### Priority 2: Evidence-backed risk findings
Inspect the current `RiskFlag` and `EvidenceSpan` schemas and `_bind_evidence` implementation.
Maintain the critical distinction between:
- A clause associated with a finding.
- A passage that directly supports a factual observation.
- A passage that supports a legal proposition.
- A model-generated suggestion that has not been verified.
- A document-level concern without a defensible local citation.
A span covering an entire clause is not proof that the clause supports the associated legal claim.
Where practical, make rule findings traceable to the specific matched text and test the offsets against the actual text representation. Preserve correct handling of segmented and cleaned clause text.
Do not upgrade an evidence status simply because a span exists.
Do not treat uncalibrated model confidence as a probability of legal correctness.
### Priority 3: False positives and missing-protection checks
Investigate whether keyword occurrence is incorrectly interpreted as the presence or absence of a contractual protection.
A term appearing only in a definition, exception, or unrelated clause may not establish that the protection exists. Conversely, the absence of a keyword does not prove the absence of a protection.
Test whole-contract behavior, including exceptions and cross-references.
Classify outcomes carefully:
- Explicit issue.
- Potential issue.
- Possible missing protection.
- Unclassified or requiring human review.
Do not equate commercial disadvantage with illegality. Do not label a missing protection illegal without a verified legal basis.
### Priority 4: Obligation extraction
Inspect extraction behavior for:
- Actor or responsible party.
- Action.
- Object.
- Trigger or condition.
- Deadline.
- Exceptions.
- Evidence location.
A deadline such as “within thirty (30) days” should not be silently dropped when the extractor can reliably identify it.
Do not invent an actor, action, or deadline when the contract does not establish one.
Use small synthetic fixtures to test precision as well as recall. Avoid expanding extraction scope without a clear evaluation method.
### Priority 5: Provenance and reproducibility
Audit the existing provenance guards and artifact manifests.
If a safe improvement is possible, make it additive and testable. A manifest must represent real source lineage, actual artifact hashes, and genuine generation details.
Keep production artifacts untouched.
Do not treat the staged A3 rebuild as production-ready merely because its row-level verifier reportedly passed.
Do not claim the previous text-overlap audit establishes a measured leakage rate. It established observed overlap requiring investigation.
## 5. Product and legal-analysis constraints
BharatContract AI is intended to help legal professionals and businesses understand contracts and identify review concerns.
Maintain these principles:
- Neutral bilateral review by default.
- Party-focused review only when the represented party is explicitly identified.
- Read provisions in their whole-contract context.
- Distinguish legal risk from commercial risk.
- Distinguish contract text from legal authority and system inference.
- Prefer verifiable primary legal sources.
- Record jurisdiction, source identity, relevant dates, and verification status when making legal-source claims.
- Preserve uncertainty and conflicting evidence.
- Do not fabricate legal-source verification.
- Do not treat “not found” as “proven absent.”
- Do not treat a missing protection as automatically illegal.
- Do not claim legal compliance merely because no rule fired.
If primary-source verification is unavailable, improve the system's uncertainty handling or document the limitation rather than inventing legal conclusions.
## 6. Engineering constraints
- Follow the repository's existing style and architecture.
- Use `uv` for Python environment and project commands.
- Avoid installing dependencies unless strictly necessary and justified.
- Do not modify dependency versions just to resolve an unrelated environment problem.
- Do not start Docker, databases, Redis, Neo4j, Celery, or external services without a clear need and explicit authorization.
- Do not add heavyweight models, external APIs, or paid infrastructure.
- Do not create new configuration systems or abstractions without a demonstrated need.
- Do not rewrite functioning components to make the code appear cleaner.
- Do not modify the frontend or add dashboards unless a demonstrated critical defect requires it.
- Do not fabricate performance gains.
- Do not commit or push changes.
Use the smallest change that addresses a verified defect.
If a task cannot be safely completed, record the blocker and move to another independently useful task.
## 7. Verification requirements
For each change, record:
1. The defect or limitation.
2. The exact files changed.
3. The regression test added or updated.
4. The command executed.
5. The actual exit code.
6. The actual test result.
7. Any limitations or untested conditions.
8. Whether the result was independently verified.
At minimum, when the environment permits, run:
- `uv run --no-sync ruff check .`
- `uv run --no-sync pytest tests/test_context_resolution.py -q`
- `uv run --no-sync pytest tests/test_evidence_binding.py -q`
- `uv run --no-sync pytest -q`
If the repository's current environment makes a command inappropriate or impossible, inspect the reason and record it. Do not silently substitute a different command and claim equivalent verification.
Run the full suite at the beginning and end when feasible. Do not spend hours rerunning an unchanged suite.
The seven provenance-related failures must remain visible unless their underlying causes are genuinely fixed with verifiable evidence. Report all remaining failures honestly.
## 8. Deliverables
Create a new report:
`reports/evaluations/autonomous_hardening_session_20261027.md`
Before creating it, check that the exact path does not already exist. If it exists, do not overwrite it; choose a unique timestamped filename instead.
The report must include:
### A. Initial state
- Actual branch and HEAD.
- Initial worktree status.
- Existing uncommitted work preserved.
- Baseline command results and timestamps, where available.
### B. Findings
- Ranked issues.
- Reproduction details.
- Root causes established versus hypotheses.
- Relevant file and test references.
### C. Changes
- Every file changed by this mission.
- Purpose of each change.
- Tests added.
- Why the change is safe.
- Any known regressions or compatibility risks.
### D. Verification
- Exact commands and exit codes.
- Actual focused and full-suite results.
- Before/after comparisons only where both were measured.
- Remaining failures and limitations.
- Relevant artifact hashes only when actually computed.
### E. Research integrity
- Data and model artifacts left untouched.
- Any provenance limitations.
- Whether any claim remains unverified.
- Whether validation was independent or only diagnostic.
### F. Next actions
- The three highest-value remaining tasks.
- Required evidence or approval for each.
- Explicitly prohibited or deferred work.
Also create a concise session log under `.commandcode/` using a new filename that does not overwrite existing files. Record completed phases, decisions, commands, and unresolved questions. Update existing project memory or decision logs only if necessary and only after inspecting their current content; preserve all previous information.
## 9. Checkpoints and stop conditions
At the end of each major phase, record a concise checkpoint containing:
- What was verified.
- What changed.
- Tests run and results.
- Remaining risks.
- The next proposed task.
If the Arbor environment supports subagents, use them selectively for independent review, test-case design, or diff review. Give each subagent a narrow task and require evidence-backed findings. Do not let parallel agents modify the same files concurrently.
If you have an independent reviewer, ask it to challenge the proposed fix and tests. Treat its response as a hypothesis until verified.
Stop and report instead of proceeding if:
- A change risks destroying or overwriting existing work.
- You cannot establish the relevant behavior from available evidence.
- A task requires production-data replacement, model retraining, a paid service, or a consequential external action.
- You find a potential security or data-integrity problem that requires explicit approval.
- You have exhausted meaningful, safely verifiable tasks.
Do not invent tasks to consume the full time budget.
## 10. Final response format
At the end of the session, provide a concise factual summary:
1. What was actually improved.
2. The most important defects discovered.

4. Whether the seven provenance failures remain.
5. Files changed and report locations.
6. Whether production data, models, or artifacts were untouched.
7. Current Git status and confirmation that nothing was committed or pushed.
8. The single best next action.
Never report success based on intended actions, partial output, or an unverified agent claim.
**Begin by recovering the project state. Proceed autonomously within these constraints, prioritize correctness over activity, and preserve every existing artifact.**

16: ## 1. What the project is
17: 
18: BharatContract AI — a college project for practical contract understanding and **evidence-grounded**
19: analysis, tuned for the Indian legal context where claims can actually be verified. Intended users:
20: legal professionals and businesses. The tool is an aid to review, **not** a substitute for legal advice.
21: 
22: **Product principles (binding):** evidence-first and traceable; cautious claims; reproducibility;
23: anti-hallucination. Review the *whole* contract (definitions, exceptions, schedules, precedence,
24: cross-references). Separate legal risk from commercial disadvantage and from drafting ambiguity. Neutral
25: bilateral review by default; party-focused review only when the represented party is identified. Separate
26: contract facts / legal authority / applicability / system inference. **A missing keyword is not proof of
27: absence; "not found" is not proof; a missing protection is not automatically illegal.** Keep serious but
28: uncertain concerns visible and qualified. Separate **severity** from **evidence strength**. Prefer primary
29: legal sources with identity, jurisdiction, effective/amendment status, relevant passage and verification
30: date; abstain when sources conflict or cannot be verified. Preserve negative results; use leakage-aware
31: evaluation. Avoid paid datasets, unnecessary infra, retraining, model proliferation and dashboard
32: expansion unless separately justified.
33: 
34: **Working style required:** read-only audit → reproducible baseline → root-cause analysis → small approved
35: changes → general-purpose regression tests → before/after evaluation → documentation + rollback. Never tune
36: to a single example contract.

196: ## 8. Provenance subsystem (implemented, verified earlier)
197: 
198: - `nlp/datasets/splits.py` — hash-pinned `SOURCE_ALLOWLIST`; `ALLOWLIST_AUTHENTICATION`
199: (`upstream_authenticated: false`, basis `internal-partition-consistency`). **Not authenticated against
200: upstream CUAD.** `resolve_source`/`resolve_split` fail closed on unknown or drifted files;
201: `validate_training_sources` rejects `test`/`mixed` unless `--allow-whole-corpus` (**documented risk**).
202: - `nlp/datasets/manifest.py` — per-artifact `<artifact>.manifest.json` (artifact sha256, sources with
203: sha256+split, code revision, command, row/label counts). `verify(deep=True)` re-hashes sources.
204: **Manifests are unsigned assertions**; only row lineage prevents a forged manifest.
205: - `nlp/provenance.py` — runtime registry; `record_loaded_model` ties status to the **exact `.joblib` bytes**
206: with `cache_sha256` drift detection; tokens `PROVENANCE_VERIFIED|UNVERIFIED|ABSENT`.
207: - `scripts/verify_provenance.py` — fail-closed readiness check = manifest **+ row-level lineage**
208: (CSV `doc_id`/`split`; JSONL `metadata.title`/`split`). `cuad_keyword_bank.json` is `lineage=none`
209: (manifest-only, aggregate structure). Exit 0 only when all pass.
210: - `tests/test_no_leakage.py` — T1a–T8 + L1–L5 adversarial (forged manifest cannot rescue an invalid artifact).
211: - `models/PROVENANCE_STATUS.json` — both `.7z` marked **`provenance-unverified`** (trained on the
212: full-CUAD corpus), with `cache_sha256` for each extracted `.joblib`.

216: ## 9. Stages P1 and P2 (implemented, not yet reviewed upstream)
217: 
218: ### P1 — deterministic context resolution
219: `resolve_context(raw_clauses) -> {clause_id: {status, references[], overrides[], related_clause_ids[],
220: unresolved_references[]}}`; reference status `resolved|missing|ambiguous`; precedence recorded as
221: **`candidate` only**. Attached as additive `Clause.context`. **Not consumed by any rule yet.**
222: Known limits: Markdown-prefixed headings (`## 7. LIABILITY`) are not parsed as section numbers, so TC-05
223: references are detected but **unresolved**; `Clause 7.4(a)`, `Section IV`, inline parenthetical
224: definitions unsupported; no inference of precedence from clause order.
225: 
226: ### P2 — evidence binding
227: `EvidenceSpan{text, char_start, char_end, clause_id, role: direct|contextual, locator: "clause_text"}`
228: and five additive `RiskFlag` fields (`source`, `evidence_status`, `evidence_spans`, `search_scope`,
229: `related_clause_ids`). Bound once in `apply_rules` via `_bind_evidence` — **no rule function edited**.
230: - Clause-level → owning clause span, `status="direct"` (**owning-clause association, not proof**).
231: - Contract-level (`clause_id=None`) → **no span**, `status="unverified"`, explicit `search_scope`.
232: - ML → `source="model"`, `status="unverified"`, `role="contextual"`; **confidence is uncalibrated**.
233: - Offsets index cleaned/segmented `Clause.text`, **not** raw file bytes.
234: - Evidence is clause-granular (rules do not expose the matched phrase).
235:

238: ## 10. Known defects / outstanding risks (ranked)
239: 
240: 1. **No whole-contract reasoning consumed yet** — context bundle exists but rules ignore it; liability-cap

243: 3. **Missing-protection = whole-corpus keyword presence** (`rules.py:~428/437`) — headings/defined terms
244: count as presence; "not found" not distinguished from absence.
245: 4. **ML booster** — model-only suggestions, uncalibrated confidence, no measured incremental value.

1: """Clause segmentation: split a contract into individual clauses.
2: 
3: Two stages:
4: 1. Rule-based heading detection (section numbers, capitalised headings)
5: 2. Sub-paragraph re-segmentation when a 'clause' is still too long.
6: 
7: The output is a list of `Clause` objects with char offsets relative to the
8: full cleaned text.
9: """
10: from __future__ import annotations
11: 
12: import re
13: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
14: from typing import List, Optional
15: 
16: from app.core.logging import get_logger
17: from app.utils.text import (
18: clean_text,
19: detect_section_number,
20: split_into_paragraphs,
21: )
22: 
23: logger = get_logger()
24:

26: @dataclass
27: class Clause:
28: """A logical clause extracted from a contract."""
29: 
30: clause_id: str
31: section_number: Optional[str]
32: heading: Optional[str]
33: text: str
34: char_start: int
35: char_end: int
36: page_hint: Optional[int] = None
37: tokens_estimate: int = 0
38: metadata: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
39: 
40: @property
41: def length(self) -> int:
42: return self.char_end - self.char_start
43: 
44: 
45: # A clause is considered "long" beyond this char count and will be split further.
46: LONG_CLAUSE_CHARS = 2500
47: 
48: 
49: def segment_clauses(
50: text: str,
51: *,
52: contract_id: str,
53: long_threshold: int = LONG_CLAUSE_CHARS,
54: ) -> List[Clause]:
55: """Split the full contract text into a list of Clauses.
56: 
57: Strategy:
58: * Group paragraphs into clauses based on numbered/heading lines.
59: * If a clause exceeds long_threshold, fall back to per-paragraph chunks.
60: """
61: if not text:
62: return []
63: 
64: cleaned = clean_text(text)
65: if not cleaned:
66: return []
67: 
68: paragraphs = split_into_paragraphs(cleaned)
69: if not paragraphs:
70: return []
71: 
72: raw_blocks = _group_paragraphs_into_clauses(paragraphs)
73: 
74: clauses: List[Clause] = []
75: cursor = 0
76: for idx, (heading, section_num, body) in enumerate(raw_blocks, start=1):
77: full_text = body if not heading else f"{heading}\n\n{body}"
78: # Find char offsets relative to the entire cleaned text.
79: start = cleaned.find(full_text[:120], cursor)
80: if start == -1:
81: start = cursor
82: end = start + len(full_text)
83: cursor = end
84: 
85: if len(full_text) > long_threshold:
86: # Split long clause into multiple sub-clauses.
87: sub_clauses = _split_long_clause(
88: body,
89: base_id=f"{contract_id}_c{idx:03d}",
90: section_number=section_num,
91: heading=heading,
92: char_start=start,
93: long_threshold=long_threshold,
94: )
95: for sc in sub_clauses:
96: sc.metadata.setdefault("parent_index", idx)
97: clauses.extend(sub_clauses)
98: else:
99: clauses.append(
100: Clause(
101: clause_id=f"{contract_id}_c{idx:03d}",
102: section_number=section_num,
103: heading=heading,
104: text=full_text.strip(),
105: char_start=start,
106: char_end=start + len(full_text.strip()),
107: tokens_estimate=_estimate_tokens(full_text),
108: )
109: )
110: 
111: logger.info("Segmented {} clauses (long_threshold={})", len(clauses), long_threshold)
112: return clauses

119: 
120: def _group_paragraphs_into_clauses(paragraphs: List[str]) -> List[tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str], str]]:
121: """Group paragraphs into (heading, section_number, body) tuples.
122: 
123: Algorithm:
124: 1. Normalise: split any paragraph that starts with a heading line and
125: has a body on subsequent lines.
126: 2. Walk through the items. Each time we see a strong-heading line
127: (starts with a section number OR is an ALL-CAPS legal title),
128: flush the current buffer as one (heading, body) pair.
129: 3. The first item is special: even if it doesn't match a heading
130: pattern, we treat it as the preamble's heading so all the
131: metadata that follows it stays attached as the preamble body.
132: """
133: grouped: List[tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str], str]] = []
134: buffer: List[str] = []
135: 
136: def _trim_heading(h: str) -> Optional[str]:
137: if not h:
138: return None
139: first_line = h.split("\n", 1)[0].strip()
140: if not first_line:
141: return None
142: if len(first_line) > 200:
143: return None
144: return first_line
145: 
146: def flush():
147: if not buffer:
148: return
149: heading_raw = buffer[0]
150: heading = _trim_heading(heading_raw)
151: body = "\n\n".join(buffer[1:]) if len(buffer) > 1 else ""
152: section_num = (
153: detect_section_number(heading.split("\n", 1)[0]) if heading else None
154: )
155: grouped.append((heading, section_num, body))
156: buffer.clear()
157: 
158: # Normalise: a heading + body joined by a single newline should be split.
159: normalised: List[str] = []
160: for p in paragraphs:
161: lines = p.split("\n")
162: first = lines[0].strip()
163: rest = "\n".join(lines[1:]).strip()
164: if _looks_like_heading_line(first) and rest:
165: normalised.append(first)
166: normalised.append(rest)
167: else:
168: normalised.append(p)
169: 
170: for idx, para in enumerate(normalised):
171: first_line = para.split("\n", 1)[0].strip()
172: # Strong heading only — not every numbered body paragraph.
173: is_strong_heading = _looks_like_heading_line(first_line)
174: if idx == 0 and not buffer:
175: # Force-start the first group with whatever we see.
176: buffer.append(para)
177: elif is_strong_heading:
178: flush()
179: buffer.append(para)
180: else:
181: buffer.append(para)
182: flush()
183: 
184: if not grouped:
185: grouped = [(None, None, p) for p in paragraphs]
186: 
187: return grouped
188: 
189: 
190: def _looks_like_heading_line(line: str) -> bool:
191: """Return True if a single line looks like a clause heading.
192: 
193: Heuristic is intentionally strict to avoid false positives on body text.
194: A line qualifies only if it has either a section number or it is short,
195: ALL-CAPS and has no terminal punctuation.
196: """
197: if not line:
198: return False
199: if len(line) > 120:
200: return False
201: # Section number ("1.", "1.1", "(a)", "Article 5", etc.). Numbered body
202: # sentences also match this regex, so reject terminal punctuation and
203: # long lines; those are clause text, not headings.
204: if detect_section_number(line):
205: stripped = line.strip()
206: if stripped.endswith((".", ",", ";", ":")):
207: return False
208: return len(stripped.split()) <= 12
209: stripped = line.strip()
210: # Strip out parenthetical / quoted segments before checking case so
211: # 'RESIDENT ACCOMMODATION SERVICE AGREEMENT ("Agreement")' is treated as
212: # an all-caps heading.
213: cleaned = re.sub(r"\([^)]*\)", "", stripped)
214: cleaned = re.sub(r'"[^"]*"', "", cleaned)
215: cleaned = re.sub(r"'[^']*'", "", cleaned).strip()
216: has_alpha = any(c.isalpha() for c in cleaned)
217: if (
218: has_alpha
219: and cleaned == cleaned.upper()
220: and len(stripped) <= 120
221: and not stripped.endswith(".")
222: and not stripped.endswith(",")
223: ):
224: return True
225: # Keyword hint — only if the line is very short (<= 60 chars) and is title-cased.
226: if (
227: len(stripped) <= 60
228: and not stripped.endswith(".")
229: and not stripped.endswith(",")
230: and not any(c.isdigit() for c in stripped)
231: and stripped == stripped.title()
232: and any(t in stripped.lower() for t in _HEADING_HINTS)
233: ):
234: return True
235: return False
236: 
237: 
238: _HEADING_HINTS = (
239: "indemnif",
240: "liability",
241: "termination",
242: "payment",
243: "confidential",
244: "data protection",
245: "intellectual property",
246: "non-compete",
247: "non-solicit",
248: "governing law",
249: "dispute",
250: "force majeure",
251: "assignment",
252: "audit",
253: "insurance",
254: "compliance",
255: "service level",
256: "sla",
257: "change control",
258: "notice",
259: "acceptance",
260: "definitions",
261: "miscellaneous",
262: "term",
263: "renewal",
264: "warranty",
265: "obligation",
266: "stamp duty",
267: "arbitration",
268: "jurisdiction",
269: "venue",
270: "severability",
271: )

273: 
274: def _split_long_clause(
275: body: str,
276: *,
277: base_id: str,
278: section_number: Optional[str],
279: heading: Optional[str],
280: char_start: int,
281: long_threshold: int = LONG_CLAUSE_CHARS,
282: ) -> List[Clause]:
283: """Split a clause body into smaller pieces at paragraph or sentence
284: boundaries.
285: 
286: Three-stage strategy:
287: 1. Group paragraphs into chunks <= long_threshold.
288: 2. If a single paragraph is still larger than the threshold, split it
289: by sentence boundaries (`.!?`).
290: 3. As a last resort, perform a fixed-character split.
291: """
292: paragraphs = split_into_paragraphs(body)
293: clauses: List[Clause] = []
294: buffer: List[str] = []
295: buffer_len = 0
296: sub_idx = 1
297: 
298: def flush():
299: nonlocal sub_idx, buffer_len
300: if not buffer:
301: return
302: joined = "\n\n".join(buffer).strip()
303: clause_id = f"{base_id}_s{sub_idx:02d}"
304: prior_len = sum(len(c.text) + 2 for c in clauses)
305: local_start = char_start + prior_len
306: clauses.append(
307: Clause(
308: clause_id=clause_id,
309: section_number=section_number,
310: heading=heading,
311: text=joined,
312: char_start=local_start,
313: char_end=local_start + len(joined),
314: tokens_estimate=_estimate_tokens(joined),
315: metadata={"split": True, "sub_index": sub_idx},
316: )
317: )
318: sub_idx += 1
319: buffer.clear()
320: buffer_len = 0
321: 
322: def _split_oversized_paragraph(p: str) -> List[str]:
323: """Split a single oversized paragraph into sentence-sized chunks."""
324: if len(p) <= long_threshold:
325: return [p]
326: # Stage 2: sentence split
327: sentence_chunks = [s for s in re.split(r"(?<=[.!?])\s+", p) if s.strip()]
328: # Stage 3: fixed-char split if a sentence alone exceeds threshold
329: out: List[str] = []
330: for s in sentence_chunks:
331: if len(s) <= long_threshold:
332: out.append(s)
333: continue
334: for i in range(0, len(s), long_threshold):
335: out.append(s[i : i + long_threshold])
336: return out or [p]
337: 
338: expanded: List[str] = []
339: for p in paragraphs:
340: if len(p) > long_threshold:
341: expanded.extend(_split_oversized_paragraph(p))
342: else:
343: expanded.append(p)
344: 
345: for p in expanded:
346: if buffer_len + len(p) > long_threshold and buffer:
347: flush()
348: buffer.append(p)
349: buffer_len += len(p)
350: flush()
351: return clauses
352:

354: _TOKEN_RE = re.compile(r"\S+")

357: def _estimate_tokens(text: str) -> int:
358: """Rough token estimate (avg 4 chars per token for English)."""
359: return max(1, len(text) // 4)

app\utils\text.py
Read 1/1 file, 105 lines
1: """Text cleaning and normalisation utilities used across the pipeline."""
2: from __future__ import annotations
3: 
4: import re
5: import unicodedata
6: from typing import List
7: 
8: # Whitespace normalisation regexes
9: _WS_RE = re.compile(r"[ \t\f\v]+")
10: _NL_RE = re.compile(r"\n{3,}")
11: _CONTROL_RE = re.compile(r"[\x00-\x08\x0b-\x1f\x7f]")
12: 
13: # Smart quote mapping (preserve readability while keeping tokens stable)
14: _SMART_QUOTE_MAP = str.maketrans({
15: "\u2018": "'", "\u2019": "'",
16: "\u201c": '"', "\u201d": '"',
17: "\u2013": "-", "\u2014": "-",
18: "\u2026": "...",
19: })
20: 
21: # Common Indian legal abbreviations and their expansions (used in token-pass hint).
22: INDIAN_LEGAL_TERMS = {
23: "MSA": "Master Services Agreement",
24: "NDA": "Non-Disclosure Agreement",
25: "SPA": "Share Purchase Agreement",
26: "SHA": "Shareholders Agreement",
27: "CIN": "Corporate Identity Number",
28: "PAN": "Permanent Account Number",
29: "GSTIN": "Goods and Services Tax Identification Number",
30: "TDS": "Tax Deducted at Source",
31: "FY": "Financial Year",
32: "INR": "Indian Rupee",
33: }
34: 
35: 
36: def normalise_whitespace(text: str) -> str:
37: """Collapse runs of whitespace and trim each line, while preserving
38: paragraph breaks (consecutive blank lines)."""
39: if not text:
40: return ""
41: text = text.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")
42: text = _CONTROL_RE.sub("", text)
43: cleaned_lines: list[str] = []
44: blanks = 0
45: for line in text.split("\n"):
46: normalised = _WS_RE.sub(" ", line).strip()
47: if not normalised:
48: blanks += 1
49: continue
50: if blanks > 0 and cleaned_lines:
51: # separator — keep as a single blank line between paragraphs.
52: cleaned_lines.append("")
53: cleaned_lines.append(normalised)
54: blanks = 0
55: return _NL_RE.sub("\n\n", "\n".join(cleaned_lines)).strip()
56: 
57: 
58: def normalise_unicode(text: str) -> str:
59: """Normalise smart quotes, dashes, ellipsis to ASCII equivalents."""
60: if not text:
61: return ""
62: text = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text)
63: return text.translate(_SMART_QUOTE_MAP)
64: 
65: 
66: def clean_text(text: str) -> str:
67: """Full cleaning pipeline: unicode -> whitespace."""
68: if not text:
69: return ""
70: return normalise_whitespace(normalise_unicode(text))
71: 
72: 
73: _SECTION_HEADING_RE = re.compile(
74: r"""^(?P<num>
75: (?:\d+(?:\.\d+)*) # 1, 1.1, 1.1.1
76: |(?:[A-Z]\.) # A.
77: |(?:\([a-z0-9]+\)) # (a), (1)
78: |(?:Article\s+\d+) # Article 5
79: )[\.\):]?\s+(?P<heading>.+)$""",
80: re.VERBOSE,
81: )
82: 
83: 
84: def looks_like_section_heading(line: str) -> bool:
85: """Heuristic check: does this line look like a clause heading?"""
86: if not line:
87: return False
88: if len(line) > 180:
89: return False
90: return bool(_SECTION_HEADING_RE.match(line.strip()))
91: 
92: 
93: def detect_section_number(line: str) -> str | None:
94: """Extract the section number if present, else None."""
95: if not line:
96: return None
97: match = _SECTION_HEADING_RE.match(line.strip())
98: return match.group("num") if match else None
99: 
100: 
101: def split_into_paragraphs(text: str) -> List[str]:
102: """Split cleaned text into non-empty paragraphs."""
103: if not text:
104: return []
105: return [p.strip() for p in re.split(r"\n\s*\n", text) if p.strip()]

READ
nlp\context\resolver.py
Read 1/1 file, 176 lines
1: """Deterministic cross-reference / context resolution for contract clauses.
2: 
3: Scope (deliberately narrow):
4: * find references to clauses/sections/schedules/exhibits/annexures/appendices;
5: * resolve them against the *current document's* parsed structure;
6: * resolve defined terms (``"Term" means ...``) across clauses;
7: * record explicit precedence/override language as a **candidate**, never as proof
8: that a conflict is resolved;
9: * report unresolved / ambiguous / missing references.
10: 
11: Nothing here decides legal meaning. The existing risk rules are untouched: this
12: module only produces a traceable context bundle for each clause.
13: """
14: from __future__ import annotations
15: 
16: import re
17: from typing import Dict, Iterable, List
18: 
19: STATUS_RESOLVED = "resolved"
20: STATUS_MISSING = "missing"
21: STATUS_AMBIGUOUS = "ambiguous"
22: STATUS_CANDIDATE = "candidate" # precedence language that references something
23: BUNDLE_COMPLETE = "complete"
24: BUNDLE_PARTIAL = "partial"
25: BUNDLE_NONE = "none"
26: 
27: _REF_RE = re.compile(
28: r"\b(?P<kind>clauses?|sections?|schedules?|exhibits?|annexures?|appendices|appendix)"
29: r"\s+(?P<nums>\d+(?:\.\d+)*(?:\s*(?:,|and|or|&|to)\s*\d+(?:\.\d+)*)*)",
30: re.IGNORECASE,
31: )
32: _DEF_RE = re.compile(
33: r"[\"\u201c](?P<term>[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9 ,/&'\-]{1,60}?)[\"\u201d]\s*(?:means|shall mean|refers to|has the meaning)",
34: re.IGNORECASE,
35: )
36: _PRECEDENCE_RE = re.compile(
37: r"\b(notwithstanding|prevails?|shall prevail|subject to|overrid(?:e|es|ing|den))\b",
38: re.IGNORECASE,
39: )
40: _SCHEDULE_HEAD_RE = re.compile(
41: r"^\s*#*\s*(?P<kind>schedule|exhibit|annexure|appendix)\s+(?P<key>[A-Za-z0-9]+)", re.IGNORECASE
42: )
43: _NUM_RE = re.compile(r"\d+(?:\.\d+)*")
44: _SENTENCE_RE = re.compile(r"(?<=[.;:])\s+|\n+")
45: 
46: 
47: def _norm_key(value: str) -> str:
48: return re.sub(r"\s+", "", (value or "").strip().lower())
49: 
50: 
51: def _sentences(text: str) -> List[str]:
52: return [s.strip() for s in _SENTENCE_RE.split(text or "") if s and s.strip()]
53: 
54: 
55: def _build_indexes(clauses: Iterable) -> dict:
56: by_section: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
57: by_sched: Dict[str, List[str]] = {}
58: definitions: Dict[str, dict] = {}
59: for cl in clauses:
60: cid = getattr(cl, "clause_id", None)
61: if not cid:
62: continue
63: sec = getattr(cl, "section_number", None)
64: if sec:
65: by_section.setdefault(_norm_key(sec), []).append(cid)
66: head = getattr(cl, "heading", None) or ""
67: m = _SCHEDULE_HEAD_RE.match(head)
68: if m:
69: by_sched.setdefault(_norm_key(m.group("key")), []).append(cid)
70: for dm in _DEF_RE.finditer(getattr(cl, "text", "") or ""):
71: term = _norm_key(dm.group("term"))
72: if term and term not in definitions:
73: definitions[term] = {"term": dm.group("term").strip(), "clause_id": cid}
74: return {"by_section": by_section, "by_sched": by_sched, "definitions": definitions}
75: 
76: 
77: def _targets(kind: str, nums: str, idx: dict) -> tuple[list[str], str]:
78: """Resolve one reference group; returns (clause_ids, status)."""
79: kind = kind.lower()
80: keys = [_norm_key(n) for n in _NUM_RE.findall(nums or "")]
81: if not keys:
82: return [], STATUS_MISSING
83: resolved: List[str] = []
84: status = STATUS_RESOLVED
85: for key in keys:
86: if kind.startswith(("schedule", "exhibit", "annex", "appendix")):
87: found = idx["by_sched"].get(key, [])
88: else:
89: found = idx["by_section"].get(key, [])
90: if not found:
91: status = STATUS_MISSING
92: continue
93: if len(found) > 1:
94: status = STATUS_AMBIGUOUS
95: resolved.extend(found)
96: continue
97: resolved.extend(found)
98: return sorted(set(resolved)), status
99: 
100: 
101: def resolve_context(clauses: Iterable) -> Dict[str, dict]:
102: """Return ``{clause_id: context_bundle}`` for every clause in the document."""
103: clauses = list(clauses)
104: idx = _build_indexes(clauses)
105: bundles: Dict[str, dict] = {}
106: 
107: for cl in clauses:
108: cid = getattr(cl, "clause_id", None)
109: if not cid:
110: continue
111: text = getattr(cl, "text", "") or ""
112: references: List[dict] = []
113: overrides: List[dict] = []
114: unresolved: List[str] = []
115: related: set[str] = set()
116: 
117: for m in _REF_RE.finditer(text):
118: raw = " ".join(m.group(0).split())
119: targets, status = _targets(m.group("kind"), m.group("nums"), idx)
120: targets = [t for t in targets if t != cid]
121: references.append(
122: {
123: "raw": raw,
124: "kind": m.group("kind").lower().rstrip("s"),
125: "target_clause_ids": targets,
126: "status": status,
127: }
128: )
129: if status == STATUS_RESOLVED and targets:
130: related.update(targets)
131: else:
132: unresolved.append(raw)
133: 
134: # Defined terms used in this clause (whole-term match, case-insensitive).
135: for _term, meta in idx["definitions"].items():
136: if meta["clause_id"] == cid:
137: continue
138: if re.search(r"(?<![a-z0-9])" + re.escape(meta["term"]).lower() + r"(?![a-z0-9])", text.lower()):
139: references.append(
140: {
141: "raw": meta["term"],
142: "kind": "definition",
143: "target_clause_ids": [meta["clause_id"]],
144: "status": STATUS_RESOLVED,
145: }
146: )
147: related.add(meta["clause_id"])
148: 
149: # Precedence language is recorded as a CANDIDATE only - never as proof.
150: for sentence in _sentences(text):
151: if not _PRECEDENCE_RE.search(sentence):
152: continue
153: for ref in [r for r in references if r["target_clause_ids"]]:
154: if ref["raw"] in sentence:
155: overrides.append(
156: {
157: "raw": " ".join(sentence.split())[:240],
158: "marker": _PRECEDENCE_RE.search(sentence).group(0).lower(),
159: "target_clause_ids": ref["target_clause_ids"],
160: "status": STATUS_CANDIDATE,
161: }
162: )
163: 
164: if references or overrides:
165: status = BUNDLE_PARTIAL if unresolved else BUNDLE_COMPLETE
166: else:
167: status = BUNDLE_NONE
168: 
169: bundles[cid] = {
170: "status": status,
171: "references": references,
172: "overrides": overrides,
173: "related_clause_ids": sorted(related),
174: "unresolved_references": unresolved,
175: }
176: return bundles

1: """Obligation extraction using spaCy dependency patterns + regex.
2: 
3: This module emits structured Obligation objects for every detected duty,
4: right, prohibition, or conditional clause. It is deliberately rule-based
5: to remain explainable and fast on the MVP; an LLM-based extractor can
6: augment this in a later phase.

9: 
10: import re
11: from dataclasses import dataclass
12: from typing import List, Optional
13: 
14: from app.models.schemas import Obligation, ObligationType, RiskSeverity
15: 
16: # Modal patterns are checked in priority order: prohibition, right, obligation.
17: _DEONTIC_PATTERNS = (
18: (
19: ObligationType.PROHIBITION,
20: re.compile(
21: r"\b(?:shall|may|must|will)\s+not\b|\bis\s+prohibited\s+from\b",
22: re.IGNORECASE,
23: ),
24: ),
25: (
26: ObligationType.RIGHT,
27: re.compile(
28: r"\b(?:is\s+entitled\s+to|has\s+the\s+right\s+to|reserves\s+the\s+right\s+to)\b"
29: r"|\b(?:may|can)\b",
30: re.IGNORECASE,
31: ),
32: ),
33: (
34: ObligationType.OBLIGATION,
35: re.compile(
36: r"\b(?:agrees\s+to|is\s+required\s+to|undertakes\s+to)\b|\b(?:shall|must|will)\b",
37: re.IGNORECASE,
38: ),
39: ),
40: )
41: 
42: DEADLINE_RE = re.compile(
43: r"\b(?:within|no later than|at least|prior to|after|by|on)\s+"
44: r"(\d+\s+(?:hour|hours|day|days|week|weeks|month|months|year|years|business\s+days))\b",
45: re.IGNORECASE,
46: )
47: DATE_DEADLINE_RE = re.compile(
48: r"\b(?:on\s+or\s+before|by|on|before|after)\s+"
49: r"(\d{1,2}\s+(?:january|february|march|april|may|june|july|august|september|october|november|december)\s+\d{4})\b",
50: re.IGNORECASE,
51: )
52: 
53: OBJECT_CUT_RE = re.compile(

55: r"pursuant\s+to|under\s+this|in\s+accordance\s+with)\b|[.;]",

57: )

79: def extract_obligations_from_clause(
80: clause_id: str,
81: clause_text: str,
82: *,
83: obligation_counter: int = 0,
84: ) -> list:
85: """Parse a clause into structured Obligation rows."""
86: if not clause_text or not clause_text.strip():
87: return []
88: 
89: # Split by sentence-like boundaries while keeping them in order
90: sentences = _split_sentences(clause_text)
91: obligations: list = []
92: for sent in sentences:
93: hit = _analyse_sentence(sent)
94: if not hit:
95: continue
96: obligation_counter += 1
97: obligations.append(
98: Obligation(
99: obligation_id=f"{clause_id}_obg{obligation_counter:03d}",
100: clause_id=clause_id,

102: obligation_type=hit.obligation_type,
103: subject=hit.subject,

108: deadline_expression=hit.deadline_expression,
109: deadline_date=hit.deadline_date,
110: risk_level=hit.risk_level,
111: confidence=hit.confidence,
112: source="rule",
113: )
114: )
115: return obligations

123: def _split_sentences(text: str) -> List[str]:
124: """Sentence splitter that respects legal abbreviations and headings."""
125: # First, split on double-newlines / headings.
126: # Strip the leading heading-like prefix (e.g. "1. Limitation of Liability").
127: text = re.sub(r"^\s*\d+(?:\.\d+)*\.?\s+[A-Z][^\n]{0,120}\n", "", text, count=1)
128: # Normalise whitespace but keep newlines as sentence boundaries for now.
129: text = re.sub(r"[ \t]+", " ", text).strip()
130: text = re.sub(r"\n+", " ", text)
131: # Protect common abbreviations
132: placeholders = {
133: "Pvt.": "Pvt\u0001",
134: "Ltd.": "Ltd\u0001",
135: "Inc.": "Inc\u0001",
136: "Corp.": "Corp\u0001",
137: "Co.": "Co\u0001",
138: "Mr.": "Mr\u0001",
139: "Ms.": "Ms\u0001",
140: "Mrs.": "Mrs\u0001",
141: "Dr.": "Dr\u0001",
142: "No.": "No\u0001",
143: "vs.": "vs\u0001",
144: "etc.": "etc\u0001",
145: "i.e.": "i\u0001e\u0001",
146: "e.g.": "e\u0001g\u0001",
147: }
148: for k, v in placeholders.items():
149: text = text.replace(k, v)
150: parts = re.split(r"(?<=[.!?])\s+(?=[A-Z(])", text)
151: sentences = []
152: for p in parts:
153: for k, v in placeholders.items():
154: p = p.replace(v, k)
155: if p.strip():
156: sentences.append(p.strip())
157: return sentences
158: 
159: 
160: def _analyse_sentence(sentence: str) -> Optional[ObligationHit]:
161: sentence = sentence.strip()
162: if not sentence:
163: return None
164: 
165: obligation_type: Optional[ObligationType] = None
166: modal_match: Optional[re.Match] = None
167: for candidate_type, pattern in _DEONTIC_PATTERNS:
168: match = pattern.search(sentence)
169: if match:
170: obligation_type = candidate_type
171: modal_match = match
172: break
173: if obligation_type is None or modal_match is None:
174: return None
175: 
176: confidence = {
177: ObligationType.OBLIGATION: 0.85,
178: ObligationType.RIGHT: 0.7,
179: ObligationType.PROHIBITION: 0.8,
180: }[obligation_type]
181: 
182: # The subject belongs to the first deontic verb, even when the sentence
183: # later contains a prohibition ("shall keep ... and shall not disclose").
184: first_modal_start = min(
185: (match.start() for _, pattern in _DEONTIC_PATTERNS if (match := pattern.search(sentence))),
186: default=modal_match.start(),
187: )
188: subject = _extract_subject(sentence[:first_modal_start])
189: raw_tail = sentence[modal_match.end() :].strip()
190: if re.match(r"be\s+", raw_tail, re.IGNORECASE) and re.search(
191: r"\b(seat|venue)\b", subject or "", re.IGNORECASE
192: ):
193: return None
194: action, obj = _extract_action_object(raw_tail)
195: if action == "governed":
196: return None
197: if action == "resolved" and subject and re.search(r"\b(seat|venue|dispute)\b", subject):
198: return None
199: condition = _extract_condition(sentence)
200: deadline_expr = _extract_deadline(sentence)
201: risk_level = _derive_risk_level(obligation_type, deadline_expr, sentence.lower())
202: 
203: return ObligationHit(
204: text=sentence,
205: obligation_type=obligation_type,
206: subject=subject,
207: counterparty=None,
208: action=action,
209: object=obj,
210: condition=condition,
211: deadline_expression=deadline_expr,
212: deadline_date=None,
213: risk_level=risk_level,
214: confidence=confidence,
215: )
216: 
217: 
218: def _extract_subject(prefix: str) -> Optional[str]:
219: subject = re.sub(r"^\s*\(?[a-z0-9]+[.)]\s*", "", prefix)
220: subject = re.sub(r"^\s*\d+(?:\.\d+)*\.?\s+", "", subject)
221: subject = re.sub(
222: r"^(?:thereafter|however|notwithstanding(?:\s+the\s+foregoing)?|further|additionally)\s+",
223: "",
224: subject.strip(),
225: flags=re.IGNORECASE,
226: )
227: subject = subject.strip().rstrip(",;:")
228: if len(subject) > 100:
229: subject = re.split(r"[,;]", subject)[-1].strip()
230: subject = re.sub(r"^(?:the|this|that|a|an)\s+", "", subject, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
231: subject = re.sub(r"\s+(?:to|of|from|in|under|pursuant|for|by)\s*$", "", subject)
232: subject = subject.strip().rstrip(",;:")
233: if len(subject) < 2:
234: return None
235: return subject[:120]

238: def _extract_action_object(rest: str) -> tuple[Optional[str], Optional[str]]:
239: rest = rest.strip().lstrip(",;: ")
240: rest = re.sub(r"^(?:not|hereby|also)\s+", "", rest, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
241: unlimited = re.match(r"have\s+(unlimited|no|full)\s+liability\b", rest, re.IGNORECASE)
242: if unlimited:
243: return "bear", f"{unlimited.group(1).lower()} liability{rest[unlimited.end():]}".strip()
244: rest = re.sub(r"^(?:be|have)\s+", "", rest, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
245: if not rest:
246: return None, None
247: 
248: match = re.match(r"([A-Za-z][A-Za-z-]{1,40})\b", rest)
249: if not match:
250: return None, None
251: action = match.group(1).lower()
252: 
253: remainder = rest[match.end() :].strip()
254: cut = OBJECT_CUT_RE.search(remainder)
255: if cut:
256: remainder = remainder[: cut.start()]
257: obj = remainder.strip(" ,;:.-")
258: return action, obj[:160] or None
259: 
260: 
261: def _extract_condition(sentence: str) -> Optional[str]:
262: m = CONDITIONAL_RE.search(sentence)
263: if not m:
264: return None
265: cond = sentence[m.start() :].strip().rstrip(".")
266: return cond[:280]
267: 
268: 
269: def _extract_deadline(sentence: str) -> Optional[str]:
270: m = DEADLINE_RE.search(sentence)
271: if m:
272: return m.group(1).strip()
273: m = DATE_DEADLINE_RE.search(sentence)
274: if m:
275: return m.group(1).strip()
276: return None

278: 
279: def _derive_risk_level(typ: ObligationType, deadline: Optional[str], sentence_low: str) -> RiskSeverity:
280: text = sentence_low
281: risk = RiskSeverity.MEDIUM if typ == ObligationType.PROHIBITION else RiskSeverity.LOW
282: if deadline:
283: m = re.match(
284: r"(\d+)\s+(hour|hours|day|days|week|weeks|month|months|year|years|business\s+days)",
285: deadline.lower(),
286: )
287: if m:
288: value = int(m.group(1))
289: unit = m.group(2)
290: if "hour" in unit:
291: days = value / 24
292: elif "week" in unit:
293: days = value * 7
294: elif "month" in unit:
295: days = value * 30
296: elif "year" in unit:
297: days = value * 365
298: else:
299: days = value
300: if days <= 1:
301: risk = RiskSeverity.CRITICAL
302: elif days <= 7:
303: risk = RiskSeverity.HIGH
304: elif days <= 30:
305: risk = RiskSeverity.MEDIUM
306: if "indemnif" in text and "all losses" in text:
307: risk = RiskSeverity.HIGH
308: return risk

The `_analyse_sentence` returns an ObligationHit with `deadline_expression=None`. But my DEADLINE_RE should match "within thirty (30) days". Let me check why it didn't match. The deadline regex: `\b(?:within|...)\s+(thirty|...)\)?\s+(?:hour|...|days)`. "within thirty (30) days" → "within " then "thirty" then " " then "(" ... wait, after "thirty" comes " (30) days". So `\s+` after the number word expects whitespace then unit, but there's " (30)" in between. My regex `\)?` only allows one optional ")" right after the number, not "(30)". 
So "thirty (30) days" doesn't match because between "thirty" and "days" there's "(30)". 
I need to allow an optional parenthetical number between the number word and the unit: `(?:\s*\(\d+\))?`. Let me update DEADLINE_RE number group.

The pattern has an extra `)`. The original structure: `r"(" + _NUM_ALT + r")?\s+(?:hour...)"`. The `r"("` opens group 1, then `_NUM_ALT`, then `r")?` closes group 1 with optional. Now I changed to `r")(?:\s*\(\d+\))?\)?\s+(?:hour...)"`. 
Breakdown of my new: `r")"` closes group 1, then `(?:\s*\(\d+\))?` optional parenthetical, then `\)?` optional close paren, then `\s+`, then `(?:hour...)`. Then the outer `r"..."` string ends. But there's an extra `)` at `business\s+days))\b` — that's the original closing of the outer `(?:hour...` group plus the `\b`. Wait the original had `business\s+days))\b` — two closing parens: one for `(?:hour...)` and one for the outer group 1 `(...)`. 
Original: `r"(" + _NUM_ALT + r")?\s+(?:hour|...|business\s+days))\b"` 
- group1: `(...)` 
- `(?:hour|...|business\s+days)` group2

## Operating instruction
You are authorized to work autonomously in the existing BharatContract AI repository while I am away from my desk.
Use the Arbor skill and Beam 1.0. Continue making useful, independently verifiable progress without asking me to approve every routine step.

## 2. Work autonomously in this order
Complete each task as far as evidence allows. After finishing or blocking a task, record its result and proceed to the next safe, useful task.
### Task A — P4: Context-aware liability and renewal rules
- Inspect and reproduce existing false positives.
- Integrate the P1 context bundle into liability and renewal rules where semantically justified.
- Test genuine concerns as well as false positives.
- Cover definitions, caps, exceptions, precedence, renewal opt-outs, reminders, and unresolved references.

### Task B — Generalize regression coverage

- Markdown headings and section references.

- Evidence binding.
- Contract-level findings.
- Obligation extraction.
- False positives caused by keywords appearing in definitions or unrelated clauses.
Add only high-value tests for demonstrated gaps. Test negative cases as well as positive ones. Avoid duplicating existing tests or overfitting one fixture.
### Task C — Evidence and finding integrity
Audit the risk-finding pipeline for cases where:
- A whole-clause span is presented as if it proves a legal claim.
- A contract-level finding lacks a defensible clause locator.
- Model-generated suggestions appear verified despite being unverified.
- Uncalibrated confidence is treated as legal certainty.
- A missing keyword is interpreted as proof that a protection is absent.
- A rule's failure to fire is interpreted as proof that a contract is safe.
Fix small, clearly established defects only. Preserve the existing meaning of evidence-status fields unless a justified, tested change is essential.
### Task D — Obligation extraction
Investigate whether actors, actions, triggers, exceptions, and deadlines are extracted correctly.
Use small synthetic examples, including ambiguous obligations and written-out dates or durations. Avoid inventing missing information. Measure precision-related false positives as well as missed obligations.
### Task E — Provenance and reproducibility audit
Inspect the seven known provenance failures and their corresponding guards.
Do not manufacture lineage, hashes, or manifests. Do not replace production artifacts. Fix a provenance issue only when its underlying cause can be genuinely resolved and independently verified.
If the only safe action is to document the missing provenance, do that and move on.
### Task F — Final integration and review
Review the complete diff, run the available verification commands, check for regressions, and create a final report.
If the tasks above are completed, identify another concrete, low-risk correctness defect in the existing implementation. Reproduce it, add a regression test, make a minimal fix, and verify it before proceeding.
Do not invent additional architecture, features, or work merely to keep busy.

Thinking
The task is very large (P4 integration of context into liability/renewal rules). Given the time and scope constraints, and that I've already done substantial work this session, I should focus on what's safely achievable. The P4 task requires integrating the context bundle (related_clause_ids) into the liability/renewal rules so that when a cap exists in a referenced clause, the false positive is suppressed.
The key insight from the handoff: `RISK_LIABILITY_002` false positive on TC-05 because the cap exists in clause 7.4 but the rule only looks at the single clause. The fix: when evaluating a clause, also check related clauses (via context) for cap language.
But I need to be careful: "Do not treat a resolved reference as proof that one provision overrides another." and "Do not silently suppress findings when context is ambiguous."
The safe approach: if the clause itself lacks cap language, check if any *related* clause (from context.related_clause_ids) contains cap language. If so, suppress the false positive but record that it was suppressed due to a related clause (not a resolved override). If context is ambiguous/missing, keep the finding.
But the rules don't currently have access to the context bundle or the clause list. The `apply_rules` function would need to pass context. Let me check apply_rules.

386: if rule.requires_indian and (
387: not summary.jurisdiction or summary.jurisdiction.lower() not in {"india", "indian"}
388: ):
389: continue
390: try:
391: flag = rule.check(cl, summary) if rule.check else None
392: except Exception:

396:

398: raw_flags.extend(_contract_level_rules(clauses, summary))

400: # 3. Dedupe by rule_id — keep highest severity + score, attach to first
401: # matching clause if available.
402: severity_order = {"critical": 4, "high": 3, "medium": 2, "low": 1}
403: by_rule: dict[str, RiskFlag] = {}
404: for f in raw_flags:
405: existing = by_rule.get(f.rule_id)
406: if existing is None:
407: by_rule[f.rule_id] = f
408: continue
409: new_sev = severity_order.get(f.severity.value, 0)
410: old_sev = severity_order.get(existing.severity.value, 0)
411: if new_sev > old_sev or (new_sev == old_sev and f.score > existing.score):
412: by_rule[f.rule_id] = f
413: 
414: # 4. Enrich each finding with its statutory / case-law pointers.
415: flags = list(by_rule.values())
416: for flag in flags:
417: if not flag.case_law:
418: flag.case_law = lookup_case_law(flag.rule_id)
419: # 5. P2 evidence binding (additive; does not alter triggers/severity).
420: _bind_evidence(flags, clauses)

Now I understand the structure. The P4 task wants me to integrate context into liability/renewal rules. The context bundle is attached to `Clause.context` (a dict). The liability rule `rule_uncapped_liability` checks if a single clause has cap language. The false positive `RISK_LIABILITY_002` fires when a Limitation-of-Liability clause lacks cap language AND no payment amount entity. But if a related clause (referenced via context) contains cap language, the finding should be suppressed.
The challenge: rules only see one clause at a time. To check related clauses, I'd need access to all clauses or the context bundle's related_clause_ids. The Clause has `.context` attribute with `related_clause_ids`. But the rule doesn't have access to the full clause list or their text.
Approach: In `apply_rules`, I could pre-compute a map of clause_id → Clause, and pass it to rules. But rules are called as `rule.check(cl, summary)`. Changing the signature would break the RiskRule contract.
Alternative: store the related clause texts in the Clause's context bundle? No, context only has IDs.
Alternative: Make `rule_uncapped_liability` look up related clauses from a module-level or passed-in registry. 
Actually, the cleanest minimal change: in `apply_rules`, before running rules, attach a helper. But rules are plain functions.

98: def _analyse(
99: full_text: str,
100: *,
101: contract_id: str,
102: title: str,
103: file_name: str,
104: warnings: List[str],
105: contract_type: ContractType,
106: user_role: PartySide,
107: jurisdiction: str,
108: ) -> AnalysisResult:
109: logger.info("Analysing contract {} ({} chars)", contract_id, len(full_text))
110: 
111: # 1. Clause segmentation
112: raw_clauses = segment_clauses(full_text, contract_id=contract_id)
113: logger.info("Segmented into {} clauses", len(raw_clauses))
114: # P1: resolve cross-references/definitions/precedence (read-only enrichment;
115: # no rule, severity or trigger behaviour is changed).
116: context_bundle = resolve_context(raw_clauses)
117: 
118: # 2. Classification + NER + obligation + risk
119: clauses: List[Clause] = []
120: all_obligations: List[Obligation] = []
121: 
122: counter = 0
123: for raw in raw_clauses:
124: cls = classify_clause(raw.text, heading=raw.heading)
125: extraction = extract_entities(raw.text, clause_text=raw.text)
126: obligations = extract_obligations_from_clause(
127: raw.clause_id,
128: raw.text,
129: obligation_counter=counter,
130: )
131: counter += len(obligations)
132: all_obligations.extend(obligations)
133: 
134: clause = Clause(
135: clause_id=raw.clause_id,
136: section_number=raw.section_number,
137: heading=raw.heading,
138: text=raw.text,
139: page_hint=raw.page_hint,
140: char_start=raw.char_start,
141: char_end=raw.char_end,
142: clause_types=cls.labels,
143: entities=extraction.entities,
144: context=context_bundle.get(raw.clause_id),
145: )
146: clauses.append(clause)
147: 
148: # 3. Summary attributes
149: summary_draft = ContractSummary(
150: contract_id=contract_id,
151: title=title,
152: file_name=file_name,
153: contract_type=contract_type,
154: jurisdiction=jurisdiction,
155: )
156: summary_draft.governing_law = detect_governing_law(full_text)
157: summary_draft.arbitration_seat = detect_arbitration_seat(full_text)
158: summary_draft.stamp_duty_mentioned = detect_stamp_duty_mention(full_text)
159: summary_draft.tds_mentioned = detect_tds_mention(full_text)
160: summary_draft.gstin_mentioned = detect_gstin_mention(full_text)
161: summary_draft.parties = _detect_parties(clauses)
162: summary_draft.effective_date = _first_date(clauses)
163: summary_draft.clause_count = len(clauses)
164: summary_draft.obligation_count = len(all_obligations)
165: 
166: # 4. Risk rules -> flags, then ML recall booster for unflagged clauses
167: flags = apply_rules(clauses, summary_draft)
168: flags = _augment_with_ml_risks(clauses, flags)
169: 
170: # 5. Annotate clauses with their own risk_score
171: for cl in clauses:
172: cl_flags = [f for f in flags if f.clause_id == cl.clause_id]
173: if cl_flags:
174: top = max(cl_flags, key=lambda f: f.score)
175: cl.risk_score = top.score
176: cl.risk_level = top.severity
177: cl.risk_reasons = [f.name for f in cl_flags]
178: else:
179: cl.risk_level = RiskSeverity.LOW
180: cl.risk_score = 0.0
181: cl.risk_reasons = []
182: 
183: # 6. Aggregate contract risk
184: from nlp.risk_scoring.scorer import score_contract
185: 
186: overall_score, overall_level = score_contract(clauses, flags)
187: summary_draft.overall_risk_score = overall_score
188: summary_draft.overall_risk_level = overall_level
189: summary_draft.high_risk_clauses = sum(
190: 1 for c in clauses if c.risk_level in (RiskSeverity.HIGH, RiskSeverity.CRITICAL)
191: )
192: summary_draft.missing_clauses = [
193: f.name
194: for f in flags
195: if f.rule_id.startswith("MISSING_CLAUSE_") or f.rule_id == "RISK_DATA_MISSING"
196: ]
197: summary_draft.top_risks = sorted(
198: flags, key=lambda f: severity_to_score(f.severity), reverse=True,
199: )[:5]
200: 
201: # 7. Surface runtime provenance verdicts (data-lineage only, not accuracy).
202: analysis_warnings = list(warnings)
203: try:
204: from nlp import provenance as _provenance
205: 
206: analysis_warnings.extend(_provenance.warnings())
207: except Exception: # pragma: no cover - provenance must never break analysis
208: pass
209: 
210: return AnalysisResult(
211: contract=summary_draft,
212: clauses=clauses,
213: obligations=all_obligations,

215: warnings=analysis_warnings,
216: )
