224: _ML_FALLBACK_SCORE = {"medium": 45.0, "high": 60.0, "critical": 75.0}
225: 
226: 
227: def _augment_with_ml_risks(clauses: List[Clause], flags: List[RiskFlag]) -> List[RiskFlag]:
228: """Add model-predicted findings for clauses the rules did not flag.
229: 
230: The ML risk classifier is a *recall booster*: it never overrides a rule, it
231: only surfaces additional candidates for human review. A no-op when no
232: trained model artefact is present, so the rule-only pipeline is unaffected.
233: """
234: from app.models.schemas import EvidenceSpan
235: from nlp.risk_scoring.risk_model import load_cached, predict_clause_risk
236: 
237: if load_cached() is None:
238: return flags
239: 
240: flagged = {f.clause_id for f in flags if f.clause_id}
241: extra: List[RiskFlag] = []
242: for clause in clauses:
243: if clause.clause_id in flagged:
244: continue
245: prediction = predict_clause_risk(clause.text)
246: if prediction is None:
247: continue
248: extra.append(
249: RiskFlag(
250: risk_id=f"{clause.clause_id}_ML_RISK_001",
251: clause_id=clause.clause_id,
252: rule_id="ML_RISK_001",
253: name=f"Model-detected {prediction.severity.value} risk",
254: severity=prediction.severity,
255: explanation=(
256: f"The ML risk classifier predicts {prediction.severity.value} severity "
257: f"(confidence {prediction.confidence:.0%}) from the clause wording, although "
258: "no explicit rule matched. Treat as a review prompt, not a definitive finding."
259: ),
260: recommendation="Manually review this clause against the standard playbook.",
261: score=_ML_FALLBACK_SCORE.get(prediction.severity.value, 45.0),
262: # P2: model-only suggestion - distinguishable and NOT evidence-backed.
263: source="model",
264: evidence_status="unverified",
265: evidence_spans=[
266: EvidenceSpan(
267: text=clause.text,
268: char_start=0,
269: char_end=len(clause.text),
270: clause_id=clause.clause_id,
271: role="contextual",
272: locator="clause_text",
273: )
274: ],
275: )
276: )
277: if extra:
278: logger.info("ML risk classifier added {} extra finding(s)", len(extra))
279: return flags + extra
280: 
281: 
282: def _detect_parties(clauses: List[Clause]) -> List[str]:
283: """Detect party names using NER entities + preamble + pattern-3 fallbacks."""
284: parties: List[str] = []
285: section_starts = re.compile(r"^\s*\d+(\.\d+)*\s*\.?\s*[A-Z]")
286: blacklist = {
287: "agreement", "indemnification", "confidentiality", "between",
288: "stamp duty", "force majeure", "term and renewal", "data protection",
289: "intellectual property", "limitation of liability", "definitions",
290: "governing law", "dispute resolution", "termination", "miscellaneous",
291: "notice", "payment terms", "warranty", "service levels",
292: "change control", "acceptance", "compliance", "insurance",
293: "audit rights", "assignment", "non-compete", "non-solicit",
294: }
295: 
296: def _scan(text: str) -> List[str]:
297: """Extract party candidates from a chunk of text."""
298: found: List[str] = []
299: if not text:
300: return found
301: # 1. Suffix-based extraction (most reliable for Indian companies).
302: pvt_suffix = re.compile(
303: r"\b((?:[A-Z][\w'.-]+\s+){0,5}(?:Pvt\.?\s*Ltd\.?|Limited|LLP|Inc\.?|Corporation|Corp\.?|LLC|PLC))\b"
304: )
305: noise_words = {
306: "having", "with", "between", "of", "the", "and", "from", "pan",
307: "cin", "gstin", "represented", "incorporated", "registered", "its",
308: "their", "whose", "whereas", "recital", "by", "as", "for",
309: "annexure", "annexed", "schedule", "section", "clause",
310: "services", "company", "agent", "customer", "resident", "client",
311: "party", "parties", "provider", "recipient",
312: "january", "february", "march", "april", "may", "june", "july",
313: "august", "september", "october", "november", "december",
314: }
315: pan_noise = re.compile(r"^[A-Z]{5}\d{4}[A-Z],?\s*(?:and\s+)?", re.IGNORECASE)
316: # Also catch "DTwelve Accommodations Private Limited" style names
317: # where "Private Limited" or "Pte Ltd" appears.
318: generic_corp = re.compile(
319: r"\b((?:[A-Z][\w'.-]+\s+){0,5}(?:Private\s+Limited|Pte\.?\s+Ltd\.?|Private\s+Ltd\.?|Ltd\.?))\b"
320: )
321: for pat in (pvt_suffix, generic_corp):
322: for match in pat.finditer(text):
323: end = match.end()
324: start = match.start()
325: prefix = text[:start]
326: cut = max(prefix.rfind("\n"), prefix.rfind(". "), prefix.rfind(" ("), -1)
327: # Walk back through tokens but stop at noise words.
328: tokens_back = re.findall(r"[A-Z][\w'.-]+", text[max(0, cut + 1) : end])
329: kept: List[str] = []
330: for tok in reversed(tokens_back):
331: if tok.lower() in noise_words or tok.lower() in blacklist:
332: break
333: kept.append(tok)
334: if len(kept) >= 7:
335: break
336: if not kept:
337: continue
338: kept.reverse()
339: name = " ".join(kept)
340: name = pan_noise.sub("", name).strip().strip(",.;:")
341: # Strip leading noise tokens like "Private Limited" that
342: # bleed in from a previous match.
343: while name:
344: first = name.split()[0].lower()
345: if first in {"private", "limited", "pvt", "llp", "inc", "corp",
346: "the", "and", "of", "company", "agent", "customer"}:
347: name = " ".join(name.split()[1:]).strip()
348: else:
349: break
350: if 4 <= len(name) <= 100 and name not in parties and name not in found:
351: found.append(name)
352: if len(found) >= 6:
353: return found
354: return found
355: 
356: # 1. Scan the heading + body of the first 3 clauses (preamble often
357: # appears as the heading of clause 0).
358: for c in clauses[:3]:
359: for chunk in ((c.heading or ""), (c.text or "")[:2000]):
360: for name in _scan(chunk):
361: if name not in parties:
362: parties.append(name)
363: if len(parties) >= 4:
364: break
365: 
366: # 2. NER entities (PARTY/ORG) from the first 6 clauses.
367: if len(parties) < 2:
368: for c in clauses[:6]:
369: for ent in c.entities:
370: if ent.label not in {"PARTY", "ORG"}:
371: continue
372: t = (ent.text or "").strip().strip(",.;:")
373: if not t or len(t) > 80 or section_starts.match(t):
374: continue
375: if t.lower() in blacklist:
376: continue
377: if re.search(r"\b[A-Z][A-Za-z]+\s+[A-Z][A-Za-z]+\b", t) and t not in parties:
378: parties.append(t)
379: if len(parties) >= 4:
380: break
381: 
382: # 3. Preamble "between X and Y" pattern (last-ditch).
383: if len(parties) < 2:
384: preamble_patterns = [
385: re.compile(
386: r"(?im)^\s*this\s+\w+\s+agreement\s*(?:\([^)]*\))?\s*is\s+(?:made|entered\s+into)?\s*"
387: r"(?:on|as\s+of)?\s*[^\n]{0,40}?\n+\s*between\s+"
388: r"([^\n,;]+?)\s*(?:\(|,|having)\s*"
389: r".*?\s*(?:and|&)\s+"
390: r"([^\n,;]+?)(?:\s*\(|\s*,|\s*having|\.|\n|$)",
391: re.DOTALL,
392: ),
393: re.compile(
394: r"(?im)between\s+([A-Z][^\n,;(]{2,80}?)\s+(?:and|&)\s+([A-Z][^\n,;(]{2,80}?)\s*(?:\(|,|\.|having|$)",
395: ),
396: ]
397: search_text = "\n".join(
398: (c.heading or "") + "\n" + (c.text or "")[:1500] for c in clauses[:2]
399: )
400: for pat in preamble_patterns:
401: m = pat.search(search_text)
402: if not m:
403: continue
404: for grp in (m.group(1), m.group(2)):
405: name = grp.strip().strip(",.;:")
406: if not name:
407: continue
408: name = re.sub(r"^\s*the\s+", "", name, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
409: drop = {"having", "with", "between", "and", "of", "the"}
410: while name and name.split()[0].lower() in drop:
411: name = " ".join(name.split()[1:])
412: while name and name.split()[-1].lower() in drop:
413: name = " ".join(name.split()[:-1])
414: if 3 <= len(name) <= 80 and name not in parties:
415: parties.append(name)
416: break
417: 
418: # Dedupe case-insensitively, prefer the most-complete spelling.
419: deduped: List[str] = []
420: seen_lower: set[str] = set()
421: for name in parties:
422: key = name.lower().strip()
423: if key in seen_lower:
424: continue
425: seen_lower.add(key)
426: deduped.append(name)
427: return deduped[:4]
428: 
429: 
430: def _first_date(clauses: List[Clause]) -> Optional[str]:
431: """Prefer entities, then generic date search."""
432: for c in clauses:
433: for ent in c.entities:
434: if ent.label == "DATE":
435: value = ent.text.strip()
436: if value and len(value) > 4:
437: return value
438: generic_date_re = re.compile(
439: r"\b(\d{1,2}\s+(?:January|February|March|April|May|June|July|August|September|October|November|December|Jan|Feb|Mar|Apr|May|Jun|Jul|Aug|Sep|Oct|Nov|Dec)\s+\d{2,4})\b",
440: re.IGNORECASE,
441: )
442: for c in clauses[:10]:
443: text = c.text or ""
444: m = generic_date_re.search(text)
445: if m:
446: return m.group(1).strip()
447: return None

The notice search in the related text failed. The regex: `notice[^.]{0,100}?(?:non-renewal|renewal|terminate)[^.]{0,100}?(\d+)\s+(hour|...)\b`. The related text is "Notice. Either party may terminate by giving 60 days notice of non-renewal." — wait, the notice regex requires "notice" first then "non-renewal|renewal|terminate" then number. But the text has "terminate by giving 60 days notice of non-renewal" — notice comes AFTER terminate, not before. So the regex won't match.
The regex pattern is `notice...terminate...number` or `notice...renewal...number`. The related text has "terminate by giving 60 days notice of non-renewal" — the number (60) is between terminate and notice, not after notice.
So the notice regex is designed for text like "notice of non-renewal ... 60 days" or "60 days notice". Let me check: the regex is `notice[^.]{0,100}?(?:non-renewal|renewal|terminate)[^.]{0,100}?(\d+)\s+(unit)`. So it needs "notice" then within 100 chars "non-renewal/renewal/terminate" then within 100 chars a number+unit.
In "Notice. Either party may terminate by giving 60 days notice of non-renewal." — there's "notice" at the start, then "terminate" appears, but then "60 days" appears before "notice of non-renewal". The regex needs number after terminate. Here number is before the second notice. So no match.
Let me adjust the related-text search to also try the reverse: search for number+unit+notice. Or simpler: just check if the related text contains a notice period pattern `(\d+)\s+(day|days|...)` near "notice". 
Actually, the cleanest fix for the test: make the related-text notice search more flexible. Let me search for `\d+\s+(?:day|days|week|weeks|month|months)\s+notice` OR `notice[^.]{0,60}?\d+\s+(?:day|days|...)`.

- Inspect `git status --short --untracked-files=all`, HEAD, and the relevant diffs.

## 2. Challenge liability suppression
Determine whether the new logic suppresses a missing-cap finding merely because a related clause contains cap language.
Test cases must include:
- A cap that clearly applies to the relevant liability.
- A cap for a different party.
- A cap for a different category of loss.
- A cap mentioned only in an unrelated definition.
- A cap that excludes the liability being assessed.
- Conflicting liability provisions.
- Ambiguous or unresolved references.
- A cap mentioned in a related clause without sufficient evidence that its scope applies.
Suppress a finding only when the available contract evidence supports the specific suppression. Reference resolution alone is insufficient.
## 3. Challenge renewal suppression
Determine whether the new logic confuses a notice period with an actual opt-out or non-renewal right.
Test cases must include:
- Explicit opt-out right with a deadline.
- Reminder obligation without an opt-out right.
- Notice period whose purpose is ambiguous.
- Notice clause unrelated to renewal.
- Conflicting renewal and termination provisions.
- Missing and unresolved references.
A reminder or generic notice period must not automatically eliminate a renewal concern.

 # 4. Risk rules -> flags, then ML recall booster for unflagged clauses
+ # P4: enrich each clause's context bundle with the text of its related
+ # clauses so risk rules can perform whole-contract (cross-reference) checks
+ # without treating a resolved reference as proof of override.
+ clause_by_id = {c.clause_id: c for c in clauses}
+ for cl in clauses:
+ ctx = cl.context
+ if not isinstance(ctx, dict):
+ continue
+ related_ids = ctx.get("related_clause_ids") or []
+ related_texts = [
+ clause_by_id[rid].text
+ for rid in related_ids
+ if rid in clause_by_id and rid != cl.clause_id
+ ]
+ ctx = dict(ctx)
+ ctx["related_clause_texts"] = related_texts
+ cl.context = ctx
+

/arbor # BharatContract AI — Provenance Guard Root-Cause Audit
Perform a narrowly scoped investigation of the seven outstanding provenance failures: T1a, T1b, T2a, T2b, T3a, T3b, and T8.
## Objective
Determine precisely what each guard checks, why it fails, whether the failure represents missing evidence or a defective check, and what a safe resolution would require.
This is primarily an audit. Do not force the suite to green.
## Procedure
1. Inspect the actual Git state and diffs. Read the existing provenance reports, guard implementations, tests, manifests, and artifact-generation scripts.
2. Run the relevant provenance tests and capture complete outputs and exit codes.
3. For each failure, record:
 - Test ID and exact assertion.
 - Expected input and actual input.
 - Missing or inconsistent provenance evidence.
 - Whether the check itself is correct.
 - The minimum evidence needed to resolve the failure.
 - Whether resolution is possible without modifying production artifacts.
4. Inspect the staged A3 manifests and lineage validation only as supporting evidence. Do not assume staged artifacts are production-ready.
5. Determine whether any guard incorrectly accepts invalid provenance, silently skips required validation, or reports an ambiguous failure.
6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.
## Required behavior
- Missing required provenance must cause an explicit failure with a clear explanation.
- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.
- Do not modify or replace `data/datasets/`, `data/processed/`, `data/processed.staging/`, models, archives, or production artifacts.
- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.
## Deliverable
Create a new report under `reports/evaluations/`, checking that the path does not already exist. Include a seven-row failure matrix, actual test output, root causes, guard correctness, resolution requirements, and any verified fixes.
Run focused tests, Ruff if code changed, and the full suite if practical.
Finish with the exact number of remaining failures, changed files, and final Git status. Distinguish verified facts from assumptions. If the provenance cannot be established, say so plainly and leave the guard failing.

57: def _norm(text: str) -> str:
58: return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text or "").strip().lower()
59: 
60: 
61: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=1)
62: def _held_docs() -> tuple[str, ...]:
63: with (CUAD / "test.json").open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
64: data = json.load(fh)
65: return tuple(
66: _norm(" ".join(p.get("context", "") for p in doc.get("paragraphs", [])))
67: for doc in data["data"]
68: )
69: 
70: 
71: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=1)
72: def _held_concat() -> str:
73: # " ||| " sentinel prevents a match spanning two documents.
74: return " ||| ".join(_held_docs())
75: 
76: 
77: def _shingles(text: str, k: int = SHINGLE) -> set[int]:
78: toks = text.split()
79: return {hash(" ".join(toks[i : i + k])) for i in range(max(0, len(toks) - k + 1))}
80: 
81: 
82: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=1)
83: def _held_index() -> frozenset[int]:
84: return frozenset(_shingles(_held_concat()))
85: 
86: 
87: def _corpus_rows(path: Path):
88: csv.field_size_limit(2**31 - 1)
89: with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
90: for idx, row in enumerate(csv.DictReader(fh)):
91: yield idx, _norm(row.get("text"))
92:

97: corpus = _require(PROCESSED / "clause_classifier.csv")
98: result = verify(corpus, allowed_splits=("train",), require=True, deep=True)
99: assert result["ok"]

101: 
102: def test_t1b_full_corpus_has_no_exact_held_out_text():
103: """Exact invariant: no corpus row occurs verbatim in a held-out document.
104: 
105: Candidate filter (exact, not approximate): containment implies the row's first
106: ``SHINGLE`` tokens occur in the held-out corpus, so rows whose first shingle is
107: absent cannot be contained. Hash collisions only add exact checks.
108: """
109: corpus = _require(PROCESSED / "clause_classifier.csv")
110: held = _held_concat()
111: index = _held_index()
112: flagged: list[tuple[int, str]] = []
113: total = 0
114: for idx, text in _corpus_rows(corpus):
115: if not text:
116: continue
117: total += 1
118: toks = text.split()
119: if len(toks) >= SHINGLE and hash(" ".join(toks[:SHINGLE])) not in index:
120: continue
121: if text in held:
122: flagged.append((idx, text[:80]))
123: assert not flagged, (
124: f"{len(flagged)}/{total} corpus rows occur verbatim in held-out documents "
125: f"(first 5: {flagged[:5]})"
126: )
127: 
128: 
129: def test_t1c_near_duplicate_heuristic_is_reported_not_asserted(capsys):
130: """Report-only heuristic. It does NOT prove absence of leakage."""
131: corpus = _require(PROCESSED / "clause_classifier.csv")
132: index = _held_index()
133: shares: list[float] = []
134: for _, text in _corpus_rows(corpus):
135: toks = text.split()
136: if len(toks) < SHINGLE:
137: continue
138: row_shingles = _shingles(text)
139: if row_shingles:
140: shares.append(len(row_shingles & index) / len(row_shingles))
141: n = len(shares)
142: mean = (sum(shares) / n) if n else 0.0
143: peak = max(shares) if shares else 0.0
144: high = sum(1 for s in shares if s >= NEAR_DUP_THRESHOLD)
145: print(
146: f"[T1c] near-duplicate heuristic (k={SHINGLE}, threshold={NEAR_DUP_THRESHOLD}): "
147: f"rows={n} mean_share={mean:.3f} max_share={peak:.3f} rows>=threshold={high}"
148: )
149: # Sanity only - this is not a leakage assertion and not a proof of absence.
150: assert 0.0 <= mean <= 1.0 and 0.0 <= peak <= 1.0

nlp\datasets\manifest.py
Read 1/1 file, 161 lines
1: """Provenance manifests for generated artifacts.
2: 
3: A manifest records where an artifact's rows came from: the source files (with
4: SHA-256 and split identity), the build command, the code revision, and the
5: artifact's own hash. ``verify`` fails closed when provenance is missing,
6: inconsistent, or derived from held-out data.
7: 
8: A manifest is a data-lineage record. It is **not** evidence of model accuracy or
9: predictive validity.
10: """
11: from __future__ import annotations
12: 
13: import json
14: from pathlib import Path
15: from typing import Iterable, Optional, Sequence
16: 
17: from nlp.datasets.splits import (
18: ALLOWLIST_AUTHENTICATION,
19: ProvenanceError,
20: normalise_path,
21: resolve_source,
22: sha256_file,
23: )
24: 
25: PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
26: MANIFEST_SUFFIX = ".manifest.json"
27: 
28: 
29: def manifest_path(artifact) -> Path:
30: return Path(str(artifact) + MANIFEST_SUFFIX)
31: 
32: 
33: def code_revision(project_root: Optional[Path] = None) -> Optional[str]:
34: """Best-effort git revision read directly from ``.git`` (no subprocess)."""
35: root = Path(project_root) if project_root else PROJECT_ROOT
36: try:
37: head = (root / ".git" / "HEAD").read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
38: if head.startswith("ref: "):
39: ref = root / ".git" / head[5:]
40: return ref.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
41: return head or None
42: except Exception:
43: return None
44: 
45: 
46: def source_record(path) -> dict:
47: """Describe a source file for a manifest (path, hash, split, doc count later)."""
48: spec = resolve_source(path)
49: return {
50: "path": spec.path,
51: "sha256": spec.sha256,
52: "split": spec.split,
53: }
54: 
55: 
56: def build_manifest(
57: artifact,
58: *,
59: sources: Sequence[dict],
60: command: Optional[str] = None,
61: row_count: Optional[int] = None,
62: label_counts: Optional[dict] = None,
63: extra: Optional[dict] = None,
64: ) -> dict:
65: """Compose a manifest dict for ``artifact`` (does not write)."""
66: artifact_path = Path(artifact)
67: manifest = {
68: "artifact": normalise_path(artifact_path),
69: "artifact_sha256": sha256_file(artifact_path) if artifact_path.exists() else None,
70: "code_revision": code_revision(),
71: "command": command,
72: "sources": list(sources),
73: "source_authentication": ALLOWLIST_AUTHENTICATION,
74: "row_count": row_count,
75: "label_counts": label_counts or {},
76: }
77: if extra:
78: manifest["extra"] = extra
79: return manifest
80: 
81: 
82: def write_manifest(artifact, manifest: dict) -> Path:
83: out = manifest_path(artifact)
84: out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
85: with out.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
86: json.dump(manifest, fh, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True)
87: fh.write("\n")
88: return out
89: 
90: 
91: def load_manifest(artifact) -> Optional[dict]:
92: out = manifest_path(artifact)
93: if not out.exists():
94: return None
95: try:
96: with out.open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
97: data = json.load(fh)
98: return data if isinstance(data, dict) else None
99: except Exception:
100: return None
101: 
102: 
103: def verify(
104: artifact,
105: *,
106: allowed_splits: Iterable[str] = ("train",),
107: require: bool = True,
108: deep: bool = True,
109: ) -> dict:
110: """Verify an artifact's provenance, failing closed.
111: 
112: Args:
113: artifact: path to the generated artifact.
114: allowed_splits: splits permitted in the manifest's sources.
115: require: when True (default) a missing manifest raises.
116: deep: when True, re-hash the source files (catches drifted inputs);
117: when False only the recorded split/hash metadata is checked.
118: """
119: artifact_path = Path(artifact)
120: allowed = frozenset(allowed_splits)
121: manifest = load_manifest(artifact_path)
122: if manifest is None:
123: if require:
124: raise ProvenanceError(f"manifest missing for {normalise_path(artifact_path)}")
125: return {"ok": False, "reason": "manifest-missing", "artifact": normalise_path(artifact_path)}
126: 
127: problems = []
128: if artifact_path.exists() and manifest.get("artifact_sha256"):
129: actual = sha256_file(artifact_path)
130: if actual != manifest["artifact_sha256"]:
131: problems.append(
132: f"artifact hash drift: expected {manifest['artifact_sha256']}, actual {actual}"
133: )
134: 
135: for source in manifest.get("sources") or []:
136: split = source.get("split")
137: if split not in allowed:
138: problems.append(f"source {source.get('path')} has split '{split}' (allowed: {sorted(allowed)})")
139: if deep:
140: try:
141: spec = resolve_source(source.get("path"))
142: except ProvenanceError as exc:
143: problems.append(str(exc))
144: continue
145: if source.get("sha256") and spec.sha256 != source.get("sha256"):
146: problems.append(
147: f"recorded source hash for {spec.path} does not match the allowlist"
148: )
149: 
150: if problems:
151: raise ProvenanceError(
152: "provenance verification failed for "
153: f"{normalise_path(artifact_path)}: " + "; ".join(problems)
154: )
155: return {
156: "ok": True,
157: "artifact": normalise_path(artifact_path),
158: "allowed_splits": sorted(allowed),
159: "deep": deep,
160: "source_count": len(manifest.get("sources") or []),
161: }

scripts\verify_provenance.py
Read 1/1 file, 187 lines
1: """Fail-closed provenance + row-level lineage verification for generated artifacts.
2: 
3: Two independent layers must both pass:
4: 1. **Manifest** - artifact hash, source hashes and split identity (may be forged).
5: 2. **Row lineage** - every row's ``doc_id``/``split`` must belong to an approved,
6: training-only document set. This is what prevents a forged manifest from
7: rescuing an artifact whose rows actually come from held-out documents.
8: 
9: Exit code 0 only when every required artifact passes *both* layers. A manifest
10: proves data lineage only - never model accuracy or predictive validity.
11: """
12: from __future__ import annotations
13: 
14: import argparse
15: import csv
16: import json
17: import sys
18: from pathlib import Path
19: from typing import Iterable
20: 
21: from nlp.datasets.manifest import verify
22: from nlp.datasets.splits import ProvenanceError
23: 
24: PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
25: CUAD_TRAIN = PROJECT_ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "cuad" / "train_separate_questions.json"
26: CUAD_TEST = PROJECT_ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "cuad" / "test.json"
27: 
28: CSV_COLUMNS = ("text", "category", "source", "doc_id", "split")
29: 
30: # Artifacts with no per-row lineage: reported explicitly, never assumed valid.
31: LINEAGE_EXEMPT = {
32: "cuad_keyword_bank.json": "aggregated keyword bank; no per-row lineage exists (manifest-only)",
33: }
34: 
35: # artifact name -> splits permitted in its sources AND in its rows
36: REQUIRED: list[tuple[str, tuple[str, ...]]] = [
37: ("clause_classifier.csv", ("train",)),
38: ("cuad_keyword_bank.json", ("train",)),
39: ("cuad_examples.jsonl", ("train",)),
40: ("maud_examples.jsonl", ("train", "dev")),
41: ("unified_examples.jsonl", ("train", "dev")),
42: ]
43: 
44: 
45: def _titles(path: Path) -> set[str]:
46: """Approved document titles, whitespace-normalised on both sides of the compare.
47: 
48: NOTE: some CUAD titles carry trailing spaces (e.g. 'Sonos, Inc. - Manufacturing
49: Agreement '), so the approved set is stripped here and row values are stripped
50: in ``_check_row`` - otherwise a valid row would fail a naive exact match.
51: """
52: with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
53: return {(d.get("title") or "").strip() for d in json.load(fh)["data"] if d.get("title")}
54: 
55: 
56: def _approved_docs() -> dict:
57: return {"train": _titles(CUAD_TRAIN), "held_out": _titles(CUAD_TEST)}
58: 
59: 
60: def _check_row(row: dict, *, idx: int, name: str, allowed: tuple[str, ...], approved: dict) -> None:
61: doc_id = (row.get("doc_id") or "").strip()
62: split = (row.get("split") or "").strip()
63: if not doc_id:
64: raise ProvenanceError(f"{name}: empty doc_id at line {idx}")
65: if split not in allowed:
66: raise ProvenanceError(f"{name}: split '{split}' not permitted at line {idx}")
67: if doc_id in approved["held_out"]:
68: raise ProvenanceError(f"{name}: held-out document '{doc_id}' at line {idx}")
69: if split == "train" and doc_id not in approved["train"]:
70: raise ProvenanceError(f"{name}: unknown training document '{doc_id}' at line {idx}")
71: 
72: 
73: def check_csv_lineage(path: Path, allowed_splits: Iterable[str]) -> dict:
74: """Validate every clause_classifier.csv row against approved documents."""
75: allowed = tuple(allowed_splits)
76: approved = _approved_docs()
77: csv.field_size_limit(2**31 - 1)
78: with path.open(encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
79: reader = csv.DictReader(fh)
80: missing = [c for c in CSV_COLUMNS if c not in (reader.fieldnames or [])]
81: if missing:
82: raise ProvenanceError(f"{path.name}: missing lineage columns {missing}")
83: rows = 0
84: for idx, row in enumerate(reader, start=2):
85: _check_row(row, idx=idx, name=path.name, allowed=allowed, approved=approved)
86: rows += 1
87: return {"kind": "csv", "rows": rows, "allowed_splits": list(allowed)}
88: 
89: 
90: def check_jsonl_lineage(path: Path, allowed_splits: Iterable[str]) -> dict:
91: """Validate CUAD/MAUD JSONL rows: split always, document id for CUAD rows."""
92: allowed = tuple(allowed_splits)
93: approved = _approved_docs()
94: rows = 0
95: with path.open(encoding="utf-8") as fh:
96: for idx, line in enumerate(fh, start=1):
97: line = line.strip()
98: if not line:
99: continue
100: record = json.loads(line)
101: meta = record.get("metadata") or {}
102: split = (meta.get("split") or "").strip()
103: source = record.get("source")
104: if source == "cuad":
105: title = meta.get("title")
106: if not title:
107: raise ProvenanceError(f"{path.name}: CUAD row without title at line {idx}")
108: _check_row(
109: {"doc_id": title, "split": split},
110: idx=idx,
111: name=path.name,
112: allowed=allowed,
113: approved=approved,
114: )
115: elif split not in allowed:
116: raise ProvenanceError(f"{path.name}: split '{split}' not permitted at line {idx}")
117: rows += 1
118: return {"kind": "jsonl", "rows": rows, "allowed_splits": list(allowed)}
119: 
120: 
121: def check_row_lineage(path: Path, allowed_splits: Iterable[str]) -> dict:
122: name = Path(path).name
123: if name.endswith(".csv"):
124: return check_csv_lineage(Path(path), allowed_splits)
125: if name.endswith(".jsonl"):
126: return check_jsonl_lineage(Path(path), allowed_splits)
127: raise ProvenanceError(f"no row-lineage checker for {name}")
128: 
129: 
130: def verify_artifact(path: Path, allowed_splits: Iterable[str]) -> dict:
131: """Manifest verification + row-level lineage. Raises ProvenanceError on failure.
132: 
133: Aggregated artifacts without rows (e.g. the keyword bank) have no per-row
134: lineage; they are explicitly reported as manifest-only rather than silently
135: treated as verified lineage.
136: """
137: artifact = Path(path)
138: allowed = tuple(allowed_splits)
139: manifest_result = verify(artifact, allowed_splits=allowed, require=True, deep=True)
140: if artifact.name in LINEAGE_EXEMPT:
141: lineage = {"kind": "none", "rows": None, "note": LINEAGE_EXEMPT[artifact.name]}
142: else:
143: lineage = check_row_lineage(artifact, allowed)
144: return {"manifest": manifest_result, "lineage": lineage}
145: 
146: 
147: def main() -> int:
148: parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Verify provenance + row lineage")
149: parser.add_argument("--out-dir", default="data/processed")
150: parser.add_argument(
151: "--allow-missing",
152: action="store_true",
153: help="skip absent artifacts explicitly (exploratory mode); never silently passes",
154: )
155: args = parser.parse_args()
156: 
157: failures = 0
158: skipped = 0
159: for name, allowed in REQUIRED:
160: path = Path(args.out_dir) / name
161: if not path.exists():
162: if args.allow_missing:
163: print(f"SKIP {path} (absent; exploratory mode)")
164: skipped += 1
165: continue
166: print(f"FAIL {path} (missing)", file=sys.stderr)
167: failures += 1
168: continue
169: try:
170: result = verify_artifact(path, allowed)
171: except ProvenanceError as exc:
172: print(f"FAIL {path}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
173: failures += 1
174: continue
175: man = result["manifest"]
176: lin = result["lineage"]
177: print(
178: f"OK {path} sources={man['source_count']} allowed={man['allowed_splits']} "
179: f"rows={lin['rows']} lineage={lin['kind']}"
180: )
181: 
182: print(f"\n{failures} failure(s), {skipped} skipped")
183: return 1 if failures else 0
184: 
185: 
186: if __name__ == "__main__":
187: raise SystemExit(main())
Thinking
The guards are correct. The production artifacts genuinely lack manifests. The resolution requires replacing production artifacts with staged ones (not authorized) or regenerating them with proper manifests. The guards themselves are correct and fail closed.

# BharatContract AI — Provenance Guard Root-Cause Audit
Perform a narrowly scoped investigation of the seven outstanding provenance failures: T1a, T1b, T2a, T2b, T3a, T3b, and T8.
## Objective
Determine precisely what each guard checks, why it fails, whether the failure represents missing evidence or a defective check, and what a safe resolution would require.
This is primarily an audit. Do not force the suite to green.
## Procedure
1. Inspect the actual Git state and diffs. Read the existing provenance reports, guard implementations, tests, manifests, and artifact-generation scripts.
2. Run the relevant provenance tests and capture complete outputs and exit codes.
3. For each failure, record:
 - Test ID and exact assertion.
 - Expected input and actual input.
 - Missing or inconsistent provenance evidence.
 - Whether the check itself is correct.
 - The minimum evidence needed to resolve the failure.
 - Whether resolution is possible without modifying production artifacts.
4. Inspect the staged A3 manifests and lineage validation only as supporting evidence. Do not assume staged artifacts are production-ready.
5. Determine whether any guard incorrectly accepts invalid provenance, silently skips required validation, or reports an ambiguous failure.
6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.
## Required behavior
- Missing required provenance must cause an explicit failure with a clear explanation.
- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.
- Do not modify or replace `data/datasets/`, `data/processed/`, `data/processed.staging/`, models, archives, or production artifacts.
- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.
## Deliverable
Create a new report under `reports/evaluations/`, checking that the path does not already exist. Include a seven-row failure matrix, actual test output, root causes, guard correctness, resolution requirements, and any verified fixes.
Run focused tests, Ruff if code changed, and the full suite if practical.
Finish with the exact number of remaining failures, changed files, and final Git status. Distinguish verified facts from assumptions. If the provenance cannot be established, say so plainly and leave the guard failing.
❯
Error: Connection error.
Type "continue" to try again. If the issue persists, contact support: https://commandcode.ai/discord
Trace ID: 6c2f60555f8c8775c140af6324ede386

2. Run the relevant provenance tests and capture complete outputs and exit codes.
3. For each failure, record:
 - Test ID and exact assertion.
 - Expected input and actual input.
 - Missing or inconsistent provenance evidence.
 - Whether the check itself is correct.
 - The minimum evidence needed to resolve the failure.
 - Whether resolution is possible without modifying production artifacts.
4. Inspect the staged A3 manifests and lineage validation only as supporting evidence. Do not assume staged artifacts are production-ready.
5. Determine whether any guard incorrectly accepts invalid provenance, silently skips required validation, or reports an ambiguous failure.
6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.
## Required behavior
- Missing required provenance must cause an explicit failure with a clear explanation.
- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.
- Do not modify or replace `data/datasets/`, `data/processed/`, `data/processed.staging/`, models, archives, or production artifacts.
- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.
## Deliverable
Create a new report under `reports/evaluations/`, checking that the path does not already exist. Include a seven-row failure matrix, actual test output, root causes, guard correctness, resolution requirements, and any verified fixes.
Run focused tests, Ruff if code changed, and the full suite if practical.
Finish with the exact number of remaining failures, changed files, and final Git status. Distinguish verified facts from assumptions. If the provenance cannot be established, say so plainly and leave the guard failing.

 - Whether resolution is possible without modifying production artifacts.
4. Inspect the staged A3 manifests and lineage validation only as supporting evidence. Do not assume staged artifacts are production-ready.
5. Determine whether any guard incorrectly accepts invalid provenance, silently skips required validation, or reports an ambiguous failure.
6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.

- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.

6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.
## Required behavior
- Missing required provenance must cause an explicit failure with a clear explanation.
- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.
- Do not modify or replace `data/datasets/`, `data/processed/`, `data/processed.staging/`, models, archives, or production artifacts.
- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.
## Deliverable
Create a new report under `reports/evaluations/`, checking that the path does not already exist. Include a seven-row failure matrix, actual test output, root causes, guard correctness, resolution requirements, and any verified fixes.
Run focused tests, Ruff if code changed, and the full suite if practical.

7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.
## Required behavior
- Missing required provenance must cause an explicit failure with a clear explanation.
- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.
- Do not modify or replace `data/datasets/`, `data/processed/`, `data/processed.staging/`, models, archives, or production artifacts.
- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.

# BharatContract AI — Provenance Guard Root-Cause Audit

 - Expected input and actual input.

 - Whether the check itself is correct.
 - The minimum evidence needed to resolve the failure.

- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.

- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.

# BharatContract AI — Provenance Guard Root-Cause Audit
Perform a narrowly scoped investigation of the seven outstanding provenance failures: T1a, T1b, T2a, T2b, T3a, T3b, and T8.
## Objective
Determine precisely what each guard checks, why it fails, whether the failure represents missing evidence or a defective check, and what a safe resolution would require.
This is primarily an audit. Do not force the suite to green.
## Procedure
1. Inspect the actual Git state and diffs. Read the existing provenance reports, guard implementations, tests, manifests, and artifact-generation scripts.
2. Run the relevant provenance tests and capture complete outputs and exit codes.
3. For each failure, record:
 - Test ID and exact assertion.
 - Expected input and actual input.
 - Missing or inconsistent provenance evidence.
 - Whether the check itself is correct.
 - The minimum evidence needed to resolve the failure.
 - Whether resolution is possible without modifying production artifacts.
4. Inspect the staged A3 manifests and lineage validation only as supporting evidence. Do not assume staged artifacts are production-ready.
5. Determine whether any guard incorrectly accepts invalid provenance, silently skips required validation, or reports an ambiguous failure.
6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.
## Required behavior
- Missing required provenance must cause an explicit failure with a clear explanation.
- Do not fabricate lineage, hashes, source identities, or generation history.
- Do not weaken assertions, skip tests, or convert required failures into warnings.
- Do not modify or replace `data/datasets/`, `data/processed/`, `data/processed.staging/`, models, archives, or production artifacts.
- Do not commit, push, reset, clean, or stash.
- Preserve every existing modification and untracked file.
- Do not start unrelated work.
## Deliverable
Create a new report under `reports/evaluations/`, checking that the path does not already exist. Include a seven-row failure matrix, actual test output, root causes, guard correctness, resolution requirements, and any verified fixes.
Run focused tests, Ruff if code changed, and the full suite if practical.
Finish with the exact number of remaining failures, changed files, and final Git status. Distinguish verified facts from assumptions. If the provenance cannot be established, say so plainly and leave the guard failing.

## Objective

This is primarily an audit. Do not force the suite to green.
## Procedure
1. Inspect the actual Git state and diffs. Read the existing provenance reports, guard implementations, tests, manifests, and artifact-generation scripts.
2. Run the relevant provenance tests and capture complete outputs and exit codes.
3. For each failure, record:
 - Test ID and exact assertion.
 - Expected input and actual input.
 - Missing or inconsistent provenance evidence.
 - Whether the check itself is correct.
 - The minimum evidence needed to resolve the failure.
 - Whether resolution is possible without modifying production artifacts.
4. Inspect the staged A3 manifests and lineage validation only as supporting evidence. Do not assume staged artifacts are production-ready.
5. Determine whether any guard incorrectly accepts invalid provenance, silently skips required validation, or reports an ambiguous failure.
6. If a guard implementation is demonstrably defective, add a focused regression test and make the smallest justified fix. If the production artifact genuinely lacks provenance, retain the failure and document the blocker.
7. Check whether test collection changed and reconcile the current test count with the previous reported runs.

102: def test_t1b_full_corpus_has_no_exact_held_out_text():
103: """Exact invariant: no corpus row occurs verbatim in a held-out document.
104: 
105: Candidate filter (exact, not approximate): containment implies the row's first
106: ``SHINGLE`` tokens occur in the held-out corpus, so rows whose first shingle is
107: absent cannot be contained. Hash collisions only add exact checks.
108: """
109: corpus = _require(PROCESSED / "clause_classifier.csv")
110: held = _held_concat()
111: index = _held_index()
112: flagged: list[tuple[int, str]] = []
113: total = 0
114: for idx, text in _corpus_rows(corpus):
115: if not text:
116: continue
117: total += 1
118: toks = text.split()
119: if len(toks) >= SHINGLE and hash(" ".join(toks[:SHINGLE])) not in index:
120: continue
121: if text in held:
122: flagged.append((idx, text[:80]))
123: assert not flagged, (
124: f"{len(flagged)}/{total} corpus rows occur verbatim in held-out documents "
125: f"(first 5: {flagged[:5]})"
126: )
127: 
128: 
129: def test_t1c_near_duplicate_heuristic_is_reported_not_asserted(capsys):
130: """Report-only heuristic. It does NOT prove absence of leakage."""
131: corpus = _require(PROCESSED / "clause_classifier.csv")
132: index = _held_index()
133: shares: list[float] = []
134: for _, text in _corpus_rows(corpus):
135: toks = text.split()
136: if len(toks) < SHINGLE:
137: continue
138: row_shingles = _shingles(text)
139: if row_shingles:
140: shares.append(len(row_shingles & index) / len(row_shingles))
141: n = len(shares)
142: mean = (sum(shares) / n) if n else 0.0
143: peak = max(shares) if shares else 0.0
144: high = sum(1 for s in shares if s >= NEAR_DUP_THRESHOLD)
145: print(
146: f"[T1c] near-duplicate heuristic (k={SHINGLE}, threshold={NEAR_DUP_THRESHOLD}): "
147: f"rows={n} mean_share={mean:.3f} max_share={peak:.3f} rows>=threshold={high}"
148: )
149: # Sanity only - this is not a leakage assertion and not a proof of absence.
150: assert 0.0 <= mean <= 1.0 and 0.0 <= peak <= 1.0
